# Engineering Backlog (Verticopolis/engine)

The backlog for this repository: follow-ups, review deferrals and ideas that are
real but not actioned in the PR that found them. Must-fix items to ship a story
belong in that story's tasks.

How items flow:

1. The review skills (`gds-code-review`, `bmad-code-review`, `*-quick-dev`)
   append each `defer` finding to the **Deferral inbox** below, under a dated
   `### Deferred from:` heading.
2. The PR that parks a `defer` triages its own new inbox entries before it
   merges: it folds each into a curated row (with a mirror issue when
   unresolved), marks it absorbed or superseded with a pointer, or retires it
   with a one-line factual reason. The inbox holds only work that has not merged.
3. **GitHub mirror.** Every curated row that is not finished has a matching open
   issue in this repository, recorded in the `GH` column as `#N`. Finished means
   `done`, `resolved`, `shipped-v1` (its remainders live on their own rows), or
   `superseded`; every other status is open work and needs a live issue. Create
   the issue when the row lands, with a plain title, a type label, the row's
   priority as a `P1`/`P2`/`P3` label and the row's notes as the body, laid out in
   the issue form the type label names. The row's `Priority` cell owns the P
   label; this repository has no label sync, so change both together. When a row
   finishes, close the issue and set the `GH` cell to `—`; when an issue closes
   first, true up the row in the next session that touches this file. A rule
   change that a frontend asked for still lands here; the frontend's own row
   points at this repository's issue.
4. `Type` legend: `review-deferral` (a real finding parked for scope),
   `perf` (a measured/suspected optimization), `feature-request` (unbuilt
   capability awaiting a spec), `bug` (a player-reported defect awaiting a
   fix), `design-decision` (a ruling recorded for the record, often parked
   with its trigger), `task` (a scoped follow-up with a known method).
   `Status`: `open`, `in-progress`, `idea` (feature not yet specced),
   `done` (delivered in full), plus the table's established refinements:
   `partial` (a named remainder is still open), `parked` (deliberately
   shelved with a resurrection condition), `next` (queued), `ready`,
   `shipped-v1` (first version shipped; the row tracks refinements),
   `resolved` (investigated or fixed and verified; kept for the record),
   `superseded`, and `impl-review` (implemented, awaiting review/validation).
5. `Priority` is the do-first order (impact × effort × risk, and whether the
   item is blocked), distinct from `Severity`, which is impact alone. **P1**:
   work next; real correctness/data-safety impact and ready to pick up. **P2**:
   worthwhile, do opportunistically when you next touch the area. **P3**:
   low/cosmetic, blocked on investigation, watch-only, or a feature awaiting a
   spec. The `Priority` value stays strictly `P1`/`P2`/`P3`. Within P3, each
   row's Notes lead with **Ready.** or **Gated.** so nobody burns a session
   grabbing a blocked "easy P3."
6. **History.** This backlog started on 2026-11-20, when each Verticopolis
   repository took its own BMAD setup. The rows and notes about this
   repository came over from Verticopolis/web's backlog with the file's git
   history. In anything dated before then, a bare `#N` means
   Verticopolis/web#N and paths are the paths of that time (the engine's were
   under `engine-rs/`, and the renderer, sprites and audio were under web's
   `src/`). The `GH` column cites this repository's issues.

| 2026-11-10 | undo-lobby-root-per-part | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **Done (2026-11-14, #1843): a step that lays ground-floor lobby tiles records the root of each part they join (`Step::lobby_roots`, `joined_roots`), and the lobby rule passes a run that reaches one that stays (`reaches_a_joined_root`). Tests: `sim_undo_parts_tests.rs`, both review cases plus one parted since that is still refused.** **Deferred from the round 10 `/gds-code-review` of the undo check follow-up to #1817 (Edge Case Hunter).** The lobby rule (`Ground::lobbies_leave_nothing_hanging` and `Ground::root_of` in `engine-rs/src/sim_undo_lobby.rs`) asks every run of ground-floor or basement tiles left beside the lobby tiles an undo takes out to reach one root, the oldest ground-floor tile that stays in their part of the tower. Where the lobby tiles joined two parts a sale had divided, a run on the other side that holds a tile built since is refused, though it stood on its own older tiles before. In play (Modern, auto-bridge off): lay lobby at 100..105, basement at (0,105..108) and lobby at 108..115, sell (0,106) and (0,107), lay lobby at (1,106) and (1,107), then build a shop at (0,110) whose dig searched for treasure, so its undo is dropped. The undo of the lobby is then refused as `Blocked` and dropped. A tile the undo lays again beside the lobby tiles brings in another part the same way (Edge Case Hunter, round 11), in play with auto-bridge off too: lay lobby at (1,100), basement at (0,100..106) and lobby at (1,104..106), sell (0,104), then in one gesture build lobby at (1,103) and sell (0,103), and build a shop at (0,107) whose dig searched for treasure, so its undo is dropped; the undo of that gesture lays (0,103) again beside the lobby tile, so the first lobby's part shares its root with the far run, and the undo is refused. Unit ids cannot tell a part parted before the lobby tiles from one parted since, which must still be refused. Fix: when a gesture lays ground-floor lobby tiles, record the root of each part they join, and pass a run that reaches any recorded root that stays. Test: those steps, expecting the undo to apply. The check errs toward refusing, so nothing hangs and no money moves wrongly. |

Cloud medians after it on the large tower: `tower_stats` 0.019 ms, `stats_report` 0.25 ms, hotspot 0.11 ms. **Update (2026-11-03, 2.78.6): the overlay extents and the attribution map.** The congestion and occupancy overlays walked every unit (tiles included, 43,358 on the large tower) at each refresh to find each floor's built extent; the extents are now kept per revision (`Simulation::floor_extents`, memo `floor_extents`, test `the_overlay_extents_follow_every_layout_edit` with a stale-cache mutant caught), and `spatial_congestion_by_floor` no longer builds and drops the attribution map. Cloud medians on the large tower, Node package, steady state: occupancy overlay 0.62 to 0.04 ms, congestion overlay 0.75 to 0.15 ms; the first read after a layout edit still walks once. Measured at the same time and left as they are: `onboarding_facts` 0.003 ms (its floor scan stops at the first upper floor tile), `stats_report` 0.26 to 0.5 ms on stats-screen open only, `peak_congestion_hotspot` 0.13 ms, satisfaction overlay 0.55 ms (the output is thousands of cells); none is a frame-budget cost on these numbers, so they wait for the PC list (#1220 successor) and come back only if it shows a long task. **Update (2026-11-03, roundtable): the onboarding floor fact** is now kept per revision (`floor_built_above_lobby`, memo `floor_above_lobby`, test `the_onboarding_floor_fact_follows_a_build_and_a_sale`), so it no longer scans every unit per UI tick. **Update (2026-11-03, roundtable): the last two items.** `stats_report` was counted again with a call counter on the large save: 14 room-index reads per open, 10 of them over every room and 4 over one kind's rooms, so about 40,000 visits on the large tower's 2,900 rooms (the earlier "about 19" was an overcount). Its budget is the panel refresh's (HP-12 in `docs/performance/hot-path-review.md`: the work scales with what is on screen and stays flat as the tower grows), so the caller reads at most once per 160 ms refresh, and a pick change marks the card for the next one (#1287 is the same fix for today's TypeScript card). **Port members (PR 1d, #1390):** `unitFacts` reads `diagnostic_facts` and inherits this gate, and `statsSheet` reads `stats_report`'s cores and inherits its repeated room passes; PR 4's call-site record carries both (`statsSheet` once per stats modal open, never per UI tick). |

Gaps still open: tier 3 is still fully `pub`, with no getters for the fields hosts read (the rest of stage 4); `log_since` returns JSON to native hosts; no Rust step driver (#867, stage 5). Closed by stages 1 to 4 part 1: the stated API boundary (`API.md`, the tier 1 pin), `frame_view`, `log_since` and `state_view` reachable outside the binding and the runner, the scenarios and engine tests that read the web game's `src/tests/fixtures/` (and `src/engine/gameplayEventBuffer.ts`), and the harness modules compiled into every host. Stage 1: `engine-rs/consumer/` (a crate depending on the engine by path that runs every tier 1 call), the `consumer` CI job (in the Rust container image with no Node.js, a sparse checkout, a copy of the consumer built outside the checkout, no `wasm-bindgen` in the native tree), `engine-rs/tests/independence.rs`, and `engine-rs/API.md` (tiers and guarantees). Stage 2 moves `frame_view`, `log_since` and the frame widths to `src/frame.rs` and `state_view` to `src/snapshot.rs` (both re-exported from their old paths) and adds `Simulation::can_build_transport`, the dry run `build_transport` now makes first (found by the stage 1 review: a host recomputed an elevator's price from the catalog); stage 3 moves the lock's seven `.vctower` fixtures to `conformance/fixtures/` (every reader repointed, file contents unchanged, so no hash moves), moves the one Rust test that read a TypeScript source (the event ring's capacity) to the TypeScript side, has the consumer replay all 22 scenarios, and runs `cargo test` and the referee in the sparse CI job (#893's prerequisite); stage 4 enforces the tiers in code (part 1 does the harness feature, the tier 1 pin, and three of the six review defers: the named frame slots, fixture resolution inside the suite, and the read-boundary guard; what is left: narrow tier 3 to `pub(crate)` or `#[doc(hidden)]` and add getters where hosts read fields, and three defers: fold the frame tests in `wasm.rs`, which run only with the `wasm` feature, into `src/frame_tests.rs` (part 1 added the named-slot test there) once #989's edits to that test module land, keeping only the binding's own checks in `wasm.rs`; give `log_since` a typed return for native hosts, where it returns JSON for the binding today; expose `can_build_transport` through the binding as a `canBuildTransport` query, which changes `src/engineBinding/engine.d.ts` and so coordinates with #946; and two from part 1's review: `tests/read_boundary.rs` accepts any number of `..` steps before `conformance`, though from `engine-rs/` one step reaches the repository root and two leave it, so count the steps against each file's base; and it skips its own file and `tests/independence.rs` whole, where each should exempt the one use by value; and it reads only `..` literals, so a path that steps up with `Path::parent`, `ancestors` or `ParentDir` is left to the sparse CI job: a text check for those spellings went through two review rounds without settling, and was taken out of part 1 by owner decision, to come back only as a parser-backed check); stage 5 is #867. Acceptance per stage in the issue. `/gds-code-review` for stages 2 to 5 (engine-data fidelity), `/bmad-code-review` for CI-only changes. |
| 2026-10-13 | engine-car-door-state | #45 | engine-rs | feature-request | P3 | low | — | open | **Post-3.0 (backlog triage 2026-10-28): listed under post-3.0 in the engine triage on #1104 (2026-10-28).** **Ready (plan `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/architecture/architecture-audio-events-2026-10-13.md`, stage 5a).** No engine output says a car stopped (`car_dwell` is private in `dispatch.rs`); guessing a stop from position and direction would re-derive an engine decision, and a boolean door flag sampled per step or frame misses stops (a 0.8-minute dwell is shorter than one sim step, which is at least one game minute). Kept off the catalog on purpose: it would flood the 1,024 ring at top speed. Internal only, no version bump (stage 5a). `/gds-code-review`. |

| 2026-10-11 | engine-vctower-byte-exact | — | engine-rs | task | P3 | low | — | resolved | **RESOLVED 2026-10-13 (v2.29.6, PR closes #939).** The engine's `.vctower` writer now writes the web writer's file byte for byte for the same serialized save, stamp and time. JSON: serde_json `preserve_order` plus `canonical::js_json`, which writes `JSON.stringify`'s text (insertion order, array-index keys first), so a save parsed from the web's JSON keeps its key order; `stamp_save` replaces existing keys in place and appends new ones, as `stamp()` does. Compression: `engine-rs/src/fflate.rs` ports fflate 0.8.3 `deflateSync` level 1 line for line (pinned by 27 reference vectors from fflate and a randomized differential), and `SaveGame.export` moved from `CompressionStream` to fflate level 1, the compressor `packSync` and `toTowerFile` already used. Pinned over every fixture in `conformanceWasmVctower.integration.test.ts`. Out of scope: a save built by the engine's own `serialize` keeps the engine's key order, since the web's order for a loaded tower follows the file it was loaded from. |
| 2026-10-11 | engine-vctower-web-writer | — | engine-rs | task | P3 | low | — | resolved | **RESOLVED 2026-10-13 (PR closes #935).** When the WASM engine runs (`?engine=wasm`), the web game writes its saves through the engine: `src/storage/vctowerWriter.ts` (`packVctower`) hands the stamped save JSON to the binding's `encodeVctower` for `SaveGame.export` and `SaveGame.packSync` (synchronous, so the pre-reload crash flush still cannot await), registered in `bootstrap.ts` once the host starts, and falls back to the fflate writer (turning the engine writer off for the session) when the engine throws or hands back text that is no `.vctower` file; a save past 8 MiB (a real tower is about 2 MB) skips the engine, whose parse and re-print near the 32 MiB cap would take seconds on the main thread and grow the shared WASM memory for good. Since #939 the engine writes the same bytes for the same stamped save, so the switch changes no file (pinned over every fixture in the WASM suite; a string holding half a surrogate pair, which the web game no longer holds, is the documented exception); no version bump, since no player can notice. Left as they are: `saveToAsync` (the timer autosave, which compresses off the main thread with `CompressionStream` and writes the same save) and `toTowerFile` (the desktop store's re-header of an already-stamped value, which the engine's writer would restamp). Package cost is unchanged by this: the served package already carries the encoder, which since #939 is `src/fflate.rs` (`flate2` only inflates). |
| 2026-10-11 | engine-save-json-limits | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | resolved | **RESOLVED 2026-10-11 (v2.29.5; engine half PR #941, web half PR #944, which closes #936).** Deferred from the `/gds-code-review` of the engine `.vctower` encoder. Engine: a lone UTF-16 surrogate escape in save JSON reads as U+FFFD through `load::parse_save_json` at every engine entry point that takes save JSON, and `encode_vctower` refuses a save whose JSON passes 32 MiB. Web: `parseSaveJson` (src/storage/saveCompression.ts) repairs the save text before parsing it with a port of the engine's `repair_lone_surrogates`, so every string and key reads the same (keys that repair to one name collapse alike, pinned across engines) where save text is read (`readSlot`, `SaveGame.import`, and the Node `decodeVctower` the harnesses use), and `wellFormed` (src/engine/wellFormed.ts) repairs a name where it is set (the tower-name callback and `Tower.setLabel`), so both engines hold one tower and the dual run no longer diverges on a split-emoji save. Every save reader holds to the one 32 MiB cap (`SaveGame.import`'s `inflate` dropped from 64 MiB) and every writer checks it through `encodeSaveJson` (`packSync`, `saveToAsync`, `export`, and `toTowerFile` outside preserve mode). |

Part 1 gave each tower a catalog handle (`towerCatalog(sim, mode?)` in `src/catalog/towerCatalog.ts` (moved out of `src/engine/` on 2026-11-03): the TypeScript tables by default, the binding while the WASM host runs the tower) and moved the price, size and rule table reads in `src/game` and `src/ui` onto it, with the patronage verdict kept on the Modern yardstick in both modes by owner decision (#572 behavior). Part 2 is split in three. **Part 2a** (#1000): colors and descriptions leave the engine data for a presentation table (`src/presentation/facilityPresentation.ts`); the view's world (lot width and floor range) is bound from the tower's catalog when the scene is built (`src/presentation/viewWorld.ts`, which throws if a later catalog states a different world), and the region grid, scenery, tile maps, camera clamps and placement read it; unit heights and car capacity in the renderer, the ground-floor rule (`catalogBuildKind`), the overhead, condo hold tax and exterminator (`catalog.economy`), and the UI's own `rentOf` calls (`unitPrice`) read the catalog; the palette rebuilds its buttons when a catalog changes the layout. **Part 2b**: add opening hours (tracked on this row; #926 has no hours item) and the household block (#926 item 2) to the catalog in both engines, then move `isOpenAt` and `hasBusinessHours` (the inspector, the editor, the sprite painters, the scene reconcile, the atlas lookup) and `householdPrice`, `residentCount` and the condo rung read `ladderRungFor` (the editor, the inspector, the stats panel) onto it. **Part 2c**: once nothing in `src/game`, `src/ui` or `src/render` reads the tables, a `no-restricted-imports` lint rule so a read cannot drift back (its by-name half for the `sim.rules` reads landed in #1813); #921 closes when that rule and the removals under **Left** are done. Still on the tables after 2a, and where each is tracked: the kind predicates (`isCommercialKind`, `isElevatorKind`, `isHotelKind`, `isLeaseAmenityKind`, `isUnmetDemandKind`, and `isRentalKind` from `src/engine/residentialRentals.ts`), the housekeeping constants (`HK_MAIDS_PER_UNIT`, `HK_NOMINAL_ROOMS_PER_MAID`, `INFEST_DAYS`), the facility diagnostics' tuning constants (with `SERVED_RECOVERY`), `GARBAGE_COLLECT_HOUR`, the traffic tier table and the `sim.rules` reads the catalog does not carry (`lobbyDistanceDrain`, `unmetDemandDrain`, `showsPreviewReason`, `hasVariantHouseholds`, `elevatorScheduleUX`), all on #926 (item 4 and the `engine-catalog-gaps` row's note; the `sim.rules` reads are invisible to an import lint rule, so 2c checks them by name, which #1813 does); the engine helpers the UI runs on the TypeScript mirror on #868; the opening hours, households and `ladderRungFor` in part 2b; the tooling handle `GameApp.grid` (`src/main.ts`, read by the e2e helpers as `g.grid.width`), which moves to the view's world in 2c; and the offline art pipeline (the atlas bake and verify in `src/render/atlas/`, the sprite gallery `src/gallery.ts`, the preview page `src/preview.ts`), which draws without a tower, binds the Modern catalog's world, and reads `FACILITIES` (names, sizes, population, attendance, the transport flag), `facilityFloors`, `subtypeListFor` and `hasHousehold`; it moves when the build can read the engine's catalog (the WASM package as a build input), before the lint rule in 2c. Settled in review: the Classic default rent needs no decision, because both engines store the Average rung at build and on load, so an unpriced unit is charged the catalog's `rent.default` (an unset Classic office reaches `rentOf`'s fallback at the same 10,000). The `isUnlocked` star gate is tracked on #868. **Original notes:** Split out of #913 (scope item 4). #913 ships the catalog: `catalogFor(mode)` in `src/engine/catalog.ts`, the same JSON from the WASM binding's `catalog(mode)` (read through `readCatalog` in `src/dualrun/catalog.ts`), both pinned by `conformance/catalog-digests.json`. The web UI still imports the TypeScript tables directly (`FACILITIES`, `ECON`, `rentConfig`, `resaleRefund`, `carResaleRefund`, `BUILD_CAPS`, `POOLED_CAPS`, `maxSpanFor`, `maxCarsFor`, `GRID`, `buildMinutes`, `subtypeListFor`, `priceOptions`) in about two dozen non-test files under `src/ui`, `src/game` and `src/render`, so those reads break when the TypeScript engine retires (roadmap row 7). Method: one catalog handle per tower (the TypeScript engine today, the binding on the WASM host), every price, size and rule read from it, presentation kept in the UI. Settle while moving: `src/game/facilityDiagnostics.ts` reads the Modern `ECON.dailyTrafficIncome` in both modes while the catalog's `dailyIncome` is mode-resolved, so the Classic customer estimate changes (likely a fix; bump if a player would notice). The money rules the UI applies itself move to engine commands in #914; this row moves only the reads. Not done in #913 because it touches render and UI code across the app and is independent of the engine API. **2026-11-10, part 2c and the bundle:** an ESLint rule (`eslint.config.js`) refuses a runtime import of `src/engine/` in any shipped file, and a production build ships no TypeScript engine module (the boot catalog check reads what the UI draws, `SaveGame.import` moved to the test tree, the tooling tower is swapped out of production builds), in #1867, merged the same day. |

| 2026-10-10 | engine-threads | — | engine-rs | feature-request | P3 | medium | — | done | **DONE (2026-11-19, closes #908 on merge of PR #2507): the worker host was deleted, the default this row's 2026-11-12 scope set, and PR #2507 removes the "worker" placement it left in the engine label, the diagnostics and the boot path.** **Rewritten 2026-11-12 (open-issue audit, product party): parallel phases dropped (#1363 closed). Measure the worker host at the hour boundary under a 4x CPU throttle, ship it only if it removes dropped frames, otherwise delete it (the default).** **Post-3.0 (backlog triage 2026-10-28): listed under post-3.0 in the engine triage on #1104 (2026-10-28).** **Ready (owner direction, 2026-10-10).** The engine runs single-threaded on the render thread. Two steps, neither changing a digest: (1) the simulation leaves the render thread: on the web the WASM engine moves into a Web Worker posting one packed snapshot per step as a transferable buffer (no SharedArrayBuffer, no cross-origin isolation headers), and a native host steps the engine on its own thread with a double-buffered frame. Today's contract is synchronous (`drainFixedSteps` re-reads the clock after every completed tick; `attachWasmHost` syncs the frame and exposes the current save at once), so the move needs a protocol first: every command and every snapshot carries a sequence number, the host acknowledges and rejects a snapshot older than the last command it sent, commands apply only at a step boundary in send order, in-flight steps are bounded (the worker never runs ahead by more than the fixed-step cap), and a save request is answered from the step after every earlier command; a test drives interleaved commands and ticks through both the synchronous and the worker host and compares digests; the measured hosted `tick(1)` gap (3.72 ms against 1.06 ms on `towerone-star4`) is mostly the boundary copy, which this removes from the frame; (2) optional parallel read-only phases behind a cargo feature on native targets, fixed partition and ordered merge, no RNG draws inside, the referee running every scenario with the feature on and off. The serial path stays the reference. Not planned: WASM threads on the web (COOP/COEP on the origin, nightly toolchain) and per-entity RNG streams (a simulation change). Sequencing: the Worker move after the 3.0.0 flip's measured release; step 2 after the TypeScript engine retires and only for phases a 5,000+ population profile shows are worth it. |
| 2026-10-09 | engine-crate-publish | #47 | engine-rs | feature-request | P3 | high | — | open | **Milestone Backlog (close-or-punt party, 2026-11-17).** **Post-3.0 (backlog triage 2026-10-28): listed under post-3.0 in the engine triage on #1104 (2026-10-28).** **P2, after the 3.0.0 flip (OD-6 (b), decided by the owner on 2026-10-20, `release-3.0-web-plan-2026-10-20.md` section 7.4; was P1): 3.0.0's major bump rests on CLAUDE.md's versioning rule, and the flip PR documents 3.0.0's compatibility policy; this row publishes the crate with its own semver policy afterward.** **Ready (owner direction, 2026-10-09).** A consumer can already depend on `engine-rs/` by a pinned git revision (`publish = false` only stops registry publication), but there is no crates.io release and the conformance lock lives outside the crate, so a frontend cannot run the referee from the package it depends on, which is how the invariant (frontends may differ, simulations may not) is proven. In order: (1) keep the crate buildable from a clean checkout at any tagged commit, so the git pin keeps working; (2) ship the lock inside the crate (`conformance/expected.json`, the scenario and fixture inputs, `conformance/loader-cases.json`, and `conformance/tdt-cases.json` once the TDT port (#881) lands it, under a `conformance` feature or an `include`), and the referee stops assuming `../conformance`; because a dependent project cannot `cargo run` a binary of one of its dependencies, expose the referee as a library entry point (`verticopolis_engine::conformance::run`, behind the `conformance` feature, returning the per-scenario results) that a consumer calls from its own test or bin, and document `cargo install verticopolis-engine --version <the same version> --features conformance --bin conformance` for running the published CLI against the version in use; (3) the crates.io lane: `publish = true`, a license field, a semver policy tied to the lock (a lock change is a breaking change), a `pub` surface audit, `cargo publish --dry-run` in CI on every PR touching the crate, a release workflow on tags; (4) a short "use the engine from another project" page naming the snapshot-in, commands-out contract and the conformance rule. Steps 2 to 4 land before the open-engine announcement. The TDT port (#881) uses the `pub(crate)` `with_thousands` from `services.rs` for one refusal message, so the crate split or this audit must make it public or keep a local copy. |
| 2026-10-08 | rng-zero-state-reload | — | engine | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **Done (2026-10-21, B6; issue #856 closes on merge): both engines restore the saved live state on load, 0 included, for the main stream (`seed`) and the event stream (`events.rngState`); a new game seeded 0 still starts at state 1.** The loader cases `seed-zero`, `seed-two-pow-32` and `events-rngstate-zero`, unit tests in both engines and `conformanceWasmLoadFidelity.integration.test.ts` pin it (`spec-gh-858-856-loader-fidelity.md`). Original row: Deferred from the `/gds-code-review` of the engine conformance suite (#854), Blind Hunter round 6. This predates that PR. `serialize` writes the main stream's live state as `seed` (`state >>> 0`), and loading builds `new RNG(seed)`, whose constructor maps 0 to 1 (`seed >>> 0 \|\| 1`). Mulberry32 passes through state 0 once per 2^32 draws, so a save taken at that moment reloads at state 1 and draws a different stream than an uninterrupted run. Odds about 1 in 4 billion per save. Fix shape: restore the saved state as-is on load, keeping the zero guard for new-game seeds. `/gds-code-review`. |

Re-scope it against the Rust code first.** **Ready.** Deferred from the `/gds-code-review` of the engine conformance suite (#854), Edge Case Hunter. This predates that PR's `setCars` carLoad fix. `setCars` truncates `carPositions` on a fleet shrink, and a rider on a removed car keeps its `carIndex`. The next motion step normally finishes that trip (`carIndex >= carPositions.length` in `src/engine/crowd/motion.ts`), but a shrink then re-grow with no motion step between (while paused) re-attaches the rider to the fresh car at the shaft's bottom. Fix shape: on shrink, finish or release crowd riders with `carIndex >= cars`, or a per-shaft car generation that motion checks. Until it lands, no conformance scenario shrinks and regrows a shaft without a `tick` between, so the suite never pins the bug. `/gds-code-review`. |

| 2026-07-31 | tdt-express-desync | — | SimTower parity | bug | P1 | high | — | done | **DONE (2026-11-04, #740 closed): the owner's PC run loaded a TOWER2 export of 24 shafts, two of them express, and the game's re-save kept all 24, both with express written last (the multi-express case this row kept open) and with express written first. Evidence: `tdt-pc-runs-2026-11-04-report.md` in the project files; findings in `docs/canon/tdt-format.md` section 9.** The rest of this row is its history while it was open, kept as written; its "stays open" lines are settled by the note above. **2026-10-09: the retail disc has no sample towers** (the disc reader check found no `.TDT` or `.TD_` file on the owner's disc), so the disc cannot supply saves for this row; it still needs game-written saves from the harness. **NARROWED 2026-08-05: no global ordering is required, and the loss scales with how EARLY the express sits.** Reordering the game's own 8-shaft fixpoint save, never altering a byte of any record: swapping two STANDARD shafts keeps **8 of 8** (and the game re-saves them in the swapped order, so the table need not be sorted by column, kind, or anything else); moving the express to index 1 keeps **4 of 8**; to index 0 keeps **2 of 8**; left at index 5 keeps all 8. Only the express's position matters, and the kept count grows with its index rather than flipping at a threshold, which argues against a validity check on slot 0 and for something cumulative in the table walk. **Express-LAST is therefore optimal, not merely safe** (it maximizes survivors), though it still cannot save a SECOND express, so the export report's warning stands and this row stays open on the mechanism. **SETTLED 2026-08-05: the trigger is the express record's POSITION in the elevator table, not its length and not its content.** Two probes from the game's own bytes closed it. (1) **Content is identical:** a game-written express record is 51 non-zero header bytes over an ENTIRELY ZERO payload (fixed block, all 8 per-floor entries, car block), and ours is byte-for-byte the same 6,274 bytes plus 26,892 more zeros from span sizing, so splicing the game's record into our file is a literal no-op; the "our zero-fill vs their live state" theory is dead. (2) **Order alone flips it:** reordering the game's own 8-shaft fixpoint save so the express sits FIRST, without altering a byte of any record, drops it to **2 of 8** kept. That explains every earlier row: express last -> everything before it survives; express at slot 5 in the game's own file -> all 8 survive; express first -> express plus one, whichever sizing. **Consequence: express-last is the best available construction of the table, not a workaround**, costing a tower with ONE express nothing. It does NOT solve #740 generally: a tower with a SECOND express still loses it and anything behind it (the 22-of-23 row), the export report still warns about that, and this row stays open. Mechanism still unknown; of the two shapes once plausible, a required record ordering is REFUTED (swapping two standard shafts is harmless and the game keeps the swap), leaving the express being read before structures a later shaft depends on. Earlier rounds (kept for auditability) retired the "8-shaft ceiling" reading via a no-express control that kept 21 of 21, and retired the whole per-floor-count family by showing the game's own record length still loses the shafts behind it. Also pinned: the game WRITES express payloads stop-sized (`194 + 3140 + 324 * inSpanStops + 348`), its re-saves carry stray stop bits outside a shaft's span so readers clamp to the span (ours does, v2.13.0), and the degraded read is a fixpoint. Game-written saves need no `COMMDLG.DLL`: a tower loaded from a path saves back over it via `File > Save`. **2026-10-29 (#1491): four real 1997 saves from u/invokes arrived (`conformance/fixtures/tdt-reddit/`).** In the 100-floor one (`TOWER2.TDT`) both express shafts (span 1 to 99, 11 and 12 stops) are written with 16 per-floor entries, not their stop count, so no documented layout sizes them and the whole elevator and stairway table was lost. The importer now falls back to finding each header by shape (with a warning); the sizing rule itself is unexplained, and the three smaller saves (express stops 4 to 14 over spans up to 69 floors) match the stop rule exactly. Open: why 16, and what the game does on load of such a file. |

A real fix needs a way to tell which hall a screen half belongs to, which the format may not carry, so measure it on the harness against a save that has two flush theatres before writing code. |

Verification wake now also covers the #572 commercial ceilings and the #573 maintenance chart. **READ 2026-07-22 (primary manual + Wine harness + independence assessment; owner call: upgrade honestly, keep open).** (1) INDEPENDENCE resolved NEGATIVE: RO, the GameFAQs FAQ lineage (BStuart ~1995 -> Aristotle47 -> furdude2 -> kiwizoid), and the Fandom wiki are ONE lineage (RO admits consulting the FAQ walkthroughs; the lineage roots in the game's own README/help + BStuart's direct play, and BStuart notes the manual's figures are wrong). Per the PR #574 ruling that is not corroboration. (2) CADENCE now primary-confirmed: the official manual, read directly off the disc (Italian full text + English OCR), states a quarter is 3 in-game days, a year is 4 quarters, Finance reports per quarter (x100); manual is SILENT on the dollar tables. (3) The rent-class STRUCTURE round-trips into the retail 1994 game under the Wine harness (`tools/simtower/build-economy-verify.ts` + `drive-inspect.sh`; a tower carrying these rungs loads and renders correctly), but a headless per-class DOLLAR read was not achievable: an imported tower's tenants never instantiate (live Pop stays 0, so a facility info window divides by zero), an all-vacant fixture crashes on load, and the info window would not open through scripted clicks. A genuinely game-populated tower needs interactive in-game building over many in-game days. (4) A second independent source, patcoston.com (outside the lineage), gives office 2k/5k/10k and condo $150k sale / $80k build. NET: office cadence primary-confirmed; office + condo values gain a genuine second source; HOTEL values remain single-lineage. Provenance notes upgraded in `src/engine/pricing.ts`, `src/engine/econConfig.ts`, the pricing GDD blockquote, and the cadence spec. REMAINING GAP (row stays open): an independent dollar read of the hotel ladders and the condo minimum. The definitive path is still the retail game (interactive build so tenants instantiate, then read the office/hotel inspector rent), or a genuinely independent primary table. |

| 2026-07-16 | weather-shapes-crowd | — | SimTower parity | feature-request | P3 | — | — | done | **DONE (v1.52.0, PR for #430).** Rain now thins the drawn crowd at the spawn layer via a new `GameRules.rainCrowdFactor()` (Classic 0.5 matching the canon shopper hit, Modern 0.7 softer), applied to the `spawnStep` accumulator in `src/engine/crowd/spawn.ts` alongside the existing time-of-day and population factors. It draws no RNG (the accumulator, not the seeded stream), so a clear or cloudy day multiplies by exactly 1 and stays byte-identical; both golden-master hashes are unchanged. A thinner crowd is what drops an attendance venue's live fill (`customersIn`), so a rainy cinema now earns less because fewer people show up; to end the double-count now that attendance income reads the live fill (#424), `rainMult` in `collectTrafficIncome` is dropped for attendance venues and kept for retail (retail income is statistical via the demand pool, not crowd-read, so the drawn crowd is not its channel; `rainMult` stays retail's one rain source). The crowd reads the authoritative `sim.weather` the loop passes in (single source shared with the income loop, so the two layers cannot disagree within a tick), falling back to the pure `weatherFor(clock.day)` hash for crowd-only paths. `/gds-code-review` ran (3 adversarial layers): one patch (single-source the crowd/economy weather read, which had skewed by one hour at each day boundary; both reviewers flagged it), the retail-double-penalty flag dismissed with evidence (retail income is statistical; `crowd.rng` is a separate RNG from `sim.rng`; golden master byte-identical), four defers recorded below. Guarded by `weatherCrowd.integration.test.ts` (crowd thins on rain; reads the passed weather not the day-hash; retail keeps rainMult at 0.5x0.6, attendance no longer pays it; a cinema fills less over a rainy afternoon end to end) and the `gameRules.test.ts` 0.5/0.7 pins. Player note in the changelog. Original context: weather affected commerce only through the `rainMult` multiplier while the crowd was weather-blind, so the visible crowd stayed full in the rain while money quietly dropped. `/gds-code-review`. |

FAR caps satisfaction (a ceiling, never evicts); VERY FAR (past the lobby-ladder reach) also erodes gently and can evict, so a skipped sky lobby costs tenants while building them every 15 floors keeps every floor capped-at-worst. The Classic/Modern split rides `GameRules.lobbyDistanceDrain` (Classic two discrete bands; Modern a smoother continuous curve, live distance in the inspector). Reuses `nearestLobbyFloorDistance`; both golden hashes re-pinned. Magnitudes provisional pending a playtest calibration pass. Shipped under `/gds-code-review`. Coverage = `min(1, 1/share)` from the statistical demand map (`computeDemandMap`, no RNG); the drain folds into the SINGLE shared placement-erosion step in `satisfaction.ts` (steepest cap, max erosion) and diverges only through `GameRules.unmetDemandDrain` (Classic cap-only; Modern cap + erosion past the evict floor). A tower with NO retail BUILT is exempt (an office building with no shops is the baseline, not a problem), keyed on the count of built operational retail venues (`retailVenueCount`, reachable or not); a tower that HAS retail penalizes a tenant that can reach none (coverage 0), including when all of its retail sits on stranded floors. Attendance venues (cinema/party hall) do not count as retail coverage (resolves the open decision on the `attendance-venue-demand` deferral note below). | 2026-07-15 | stairs-willingness-canon | — | SimTower parity | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **CLASSIC ENFORCEMENT SHIPPED 2026-07-19 (v1.65.0, PR #501; #384 closed on merge). Follow-ups #502 (Modern penalty) and #503 (MAX_RIDES) carry the remaining work on their own rows.** The party's re-scope was implemented as spec'd: `bfsRouteExpressGated` (Classic passengers) rewritten from a level-BFS to a fewest-edges BFS with state `(floor, arrived-by-express, rides, walkRun, runCap)`. Stair/escalator edges (tagged `walkKind` in `buildAdjacency`) spend a SEPARATE contiguous-walk budget (stairs 4, escalators 7; stricter governs a mixed run) that resets to zero on boarding any car, so pure-stair reach LOOSENS from 2 to 4 as canon requires; the ride budget stays `MAX_RIDES = 2` and the #396 express-transfer gate still applies to a walk off an express arrival. `bfsRoute` (Modern + all staff routing) untouched. Design authored via `gds-gdd` (parity GDD §8) + spec; `/gds-code-review` clean (composite-key optimality, 4/7 boundary, determinism, O(E) bounded-constant, applied fail-closed guard + tightened types). TDT parity: willingness is live routing, NOT persisted; export byte-identical (404532 B) and the Wine carryover renders unchanged. TWO FOLLOW-UPS spun out (both owner-gated, do NOT ship autonomously): (a) **Modern comfort-penalty** at the same knee (climb allowed, tenant unhappy, eventually leaves) as a separate satisfaction-model track (GDD epic E6, own Samus tuning pass) - see row `modern-walkway-comfort-penalty`; (b) **`MAX_RIDES = 2` may be too small** for tall multi-transfer elevator journeys (express -> sky-lobby-standard -> local), a possible SECOND bug independent of stairs (GDD open question 11.4) - see row `max-rides-multitransfer-budget`. OUTCOME: PR #501 merged (v1.65.0); the follow-up rows #502 and #503 carry the remainder. Prior ruling retained below. **CANON RATIFIED 2026-07-19 (web research + design party): STAIRS = 4, ESCALATORS = 7 contiguous flights per trip (an elevator ride resets the count).** Sources converge (roadwolf "4 sets of stairs per trip"; relentlessoptimizer "maximum of four flights"; GameFAQs); the lone "5 levels" counts the origin floor. SHIPPED as canon: `WALKWAY_WILLINGNESS` in `src/engine/facilityCaps.ts` + a canon test + PARITY.md note (the constant landed in PR #499; Classic enforcement then shipped in PR #501). **PARTY RULING on enforcement (owner greenlight required, does NOT ship autonomously):** it splits by mode. **Classic** = a HARD refusal past the threshold (the 1994 wall) counting contiguous walk legs with an elevator resetting the count; small scope (one router branch + the constant + a test). **Modern** = a SOFT satisfaction/comfort penalty at the SAME threshold (climb allowed, tenant unhappy, pays less, eventually leaves) rather than a refusal, because uncapped stairs are a dominant strategy that deletes the elevator game; larger scope (a new term in the tenant-satisfaction model, its own GDD + Samus tuning pass) and MUST NOT block the Classic cap. Both rulesets read the one `WALKWAY_WILLINGNESS` constant. **CORRECTION 2026-07-19 (verified by probing `bfsRoute` directly): the earlier "engine has NO cap" premise is WRONG.** The router caps a whole trip at `MAX_RIDES` = 2 boardings, and each single stair flight is one boarding, so pure-stair reach is currently **2 floors** (a 3-flight climb is REFUSED today), STRICTER than the canon 4, and stairs share that budget with elevators (no separate walk budget; a stair after an express->standard journey is already refused). So matching canon is NOT "add a cap" (more restrictive) but "give walk legs their OWN contiguous budget of 4/7 separate from the 2-ride elevator budget, reset by an elevator ride" (LOOSENS stair reach). That is a bigger BFS restructuring than the party scoped ("one router branch"), and it FLIPS the design premise the party + owner greenlight were based on: the owner should re-decide with these correct facts before implementation. OUTCOME: the Classic cap shipped (spec + `/gds-code-review` + v1.65.0, PR #501); the Modern comfort-penalty GDD is deferred to #502. Original: **RE-SCOPED 2026-07-19 (harness session): this is NOT a quick "pin an existing constant".** Two findings: (1) our engine does NOT model a cumulative stair-climb willingness cap at all - `src/engine/crowd/routing.ts` treats stairs/escalators as free walkable graph links with no floor-count limit (consistent with PARITY.md's "our staff routing has no such degradation"). So adding the cap is a genuine GAMEPLAY change (Samus feel-check + `/gds-code-review` + version bump), not a constant tweak. (2) The 4-vs-5 canon value can't be settled cheaply on the headless harness: it needs a behavioral study (build a stairs-only reach, run the sim, read tenant stress over time), not a single screenshot; the on-disc manual would state it but is game IP we keep out of git. NEXT: decide whether Modern/Classic should even model stair willingness before RE'ing the exact number; if yes, a dedicated behavioral harness run or an owner-ratified canon value. Original: **Gated: canon verification via the Wine harness. From gdd-simtower-parity-2026-07-06.** Sources conflict on how many cumulative floors a person will climb by stairs (4 vs 5). Verify in the real game and pin the constant; same species as walkway-variants. |

(1) The wait-based signal (#514's crowd EMA) is cadence-dependent, so feeding it into persisted satisfaction breaks headless/browser determinism (proven: a Modern tower's office satisfactions diverged across tick rates, `[…0.98,0.87]` coarse vs `[…0.66,0.59]` fine; Classic identical). So the crowd accumulator must stay read-only. (2) The only deterministic substitute, the forced stair-climb length to a floor, is redundant with the existing `lobbyFar` drain (a long forced climb IS being far from a lobby) and elevator-WAIT is already the `congestion` drain's job; in testing the penalty always resolved to the sharper `lobbyFar` gripe. The behavioral refinement (make the lobby-distance drain's advice correct on a stair-only floor: "run an elevator here", not "add a sky lobby") moves to **#436**. Spec: `spec-modern-commute-comfort.md`. Prior rulings retained below. Absorbs the deferred #384-Modern walkway penalty and the held-back Classic/Modern comparison line (compare.ts). Modern's track: accumulate a per-tenant commute discomfort from the tenant's actual wait/travel time (reusing the per-person `wait` already computed), surface it ("this tenant's commute is rough"), and feed a GENTLE satisfaction nudge. Also absorbs the deferred #384 Modern walkway penalty (one "commute/vertical hassle" term feeding both stair flights and elevator wait) and closes the per-Sim-stress gap #514 for Modern legibly. HARD GUARDRAIL: never a reachability wall (grumble + slow attrition only, never "won't move in", or it is `MAX_RIDES` reborn). Owner-tuned curve. Modern was left out of scope. Party ruling / GDD epic E6: Modern applies a comfort/satisfaction penalty at the SAME knee (climb allowed, tenant unhappy, pays less, eventually leaves) rather than a hard refusal, because uncapped stairs are a dominant strategy that deletes the elevator game. Owner-gated, does NOT ship autonomously: a new term in the tenant-satisfaction model + its own Samus tuning pass (own GDD track, not a router branch). Seam exists (`sim.rules` / `GameRules`; no `mode === "modern"` outside gameRules.ts); reads the same `WALKWAY_WILLINGNESS` constant as Classic. |
| 2026-07-19 | max-rides-multitransfer-budget | — | SimTower parity | bug | P3 | med | — | done | **SHIPPED 2026-07-20 (v1.67.0; #503 closes on merge). Party ruled Classic to FULL PARITY: reachability uncapped in BOTH modes.** The harness measured the real ceiling at 6+ transfers (4-zone then 6-zone discriminator towers all rented their top band from empty), i.e. the 1994 original has effectively NO reachability ride cap. Owner directed "make classic at parity"; the game/design party ratified uncapping BOTH modes (Modern must never be more restrictive than Classic). Implemented: `MAX_RIDES` removed, `bfsRoute` uncapped, Classic keeps the #384 walk budget (renamed `bfsRouteWalkBudget`), Modern applies no hard walk budget. The cost of a bad layout is now the faithful wait-time/congestion satisfaction model (research: the original penalized elevator WAIT TIME, `Quality = 300 - total stress/tenants`, not transfer count), not a hard wall. Bundled with #509 (same router). Prior CONFIRMED note retained below. **CONFIRMED via Wine harness 2026-07-20: the 1994 original routes >= 3 elevator transfers, so `MAX_RIDES = 2` is a real under-service bug.** Discriminator tower (`tools/simtower/build-multitransfer-503.ts`): three zoned standard shafts (1-4, 4-7, 7-10), no express/stairs, so floor 9 needs exactly 3 boardings; our router refuses band3 while band1/band2 pass. Loaded in the real game two ways: seeded-occupied band3 held its tenants through Year 3 (unreachable offices evict in weeks), and started-empty band3 rented out on its own by Year 4 (a tenant only moves into a reachable office). Both agree: the original completes the 3-transfer commute. Proves budget >= 3; exact ceiling (4+?) not pinned (a 4-zone tower would measure it). FIX stays owner-gated (golden-master-affecting in BOTH modes): own spec + `/gds-code-review` + version bump + a decision on the new budget value; do NOT bump `MAX_RIDES` autonomously. Harness gained a saved-camera-view export (frames the .TDT on load) + env-tunable zoom-click coords. Original: **Spun out of #384 2026-07-19 (GDD open question 11.4); independent of stairs, a possible SECOND routing bug.** The passenger router caps a trip at `MAX_RIDES = 2` elevator boardings (`src/engine/crowd/routing.ts`). A tall tower's intended journey can need three legs (express to a sky lobby, a standard to an intermediate sky lobby, then a local to the destination), which is 3 boardings and refused today even though the original routes such trips. The #384 work only gave WALKS their own budget separate from the ride budget; the ride budget itself was left at 2, unchanged. Bumping `MAX_RIDES` is a golden-master-affecting reachability change in BOTH modes and needs its own investigation + spec + `/gds-code-review` + version bump. NEXT: Wine-harness check whether a 3-transfer elevator journey is actually routed in the 1994 original, establish the correct budget, then spec. Do NOT silently bump `MAX_RIDES`. |

Re-scope it against the Rust code first.** **PARTY RULING 2026-07-20 (game/dev/design/player roundtable): ADOPT as the shared substrate; re-scoped.** This is Classic parity fidelity (the 1994 `Quality = 300 - stress/tenant` model IS per-Sim), so BOTH modes read it, but its standalone play value is near-zero (only the pathological lone-far-tenant case); its real purpose is to FEED #502. Split by risk: (a) BUILDABLE now, safe: a per-origin-unit commute-stress accumulator off the per-person `wait` we already compute, exposed READ-ONLY with no behavior change, hot-path benchmarked FIRST (measure-before-per-frame, cf #338), deterministic, golden-tested; (b) OWNER-GATED: any coupling of that number into satisfaction/attrition (that lands in #502's curve). Sequenced BEFORE #502. Next artifact: a `bmad-spec` for the accumulator, then a `/gds-code-review`-gated implementation. Original: **Surfaced by the game/design party 2026-07-20 ratifying the routing parity (#503/#509).** The 1994 original accumulates stress PER-SIM from elevator wait time across a whole commute (`Quality = 300 - total stress/tenants`). Our engine models the cost as tower-wide `crowd.frustration` + per-floor `congestion` feeding gripes/move-outs, a faithful AGGREGATE proxy but not the original's per-Sim accumulation. The gap only bites an edge case: a lone far tenant whose multi-transfer commute is slow while the rest of the tower's elevators sit idle (congestion ~0, frustration ~0, so that tenant is under-stressed). PRE-EXISTING (existed at MAX_RIDES=2; uncapping merely exposes it since far tenants can now move in), so NOT a blocker for the parity fix. Fidelity refinement: accumulate a per-origin-unit commute stress from the per-person `wait` we already compute. Both modes benefit; Modern's legible version is the #502 track. |

| 2026-07-13 | tdt-floor1213-skygap | — | SimTower parity | bug | P3 | low | — | done | **DONE 2026-07-19 (v1.64.0, PR #492; #318 closed on merge). Cause is record ORDER, not the remap.** The exporter appended each floor's empty-floor (type-0) paving spans AFTER the room records, so a wide floor with a mid-floor gap emitted its records out of ascending-x order. The real 1994 renderer walks a floor's records left to right and TRUNCATES the floor at the first record whose left edge goes backwards, drawing everything past it as bare sky. On a dense imported tower most floors carry a gap, so the symptom is tower-wide, not just floors 12/13. Proven in the Wine harness with a controlled repro (alternating gap/no-gap office floors: gap floors lost their right half, no-gap floors rendered full) and confirmed on the owner's sixseven_14 (floors 2-11 went from empty sky to fully populated). The remap-table theory was DISPROVEN by experiment: populating the 94xu16 remap did NOT fix the truncation, and a real save (`my_tower`) renders correctly on record order alone. Independently confirmed by the save-in-game round-trip: the real game's own re-save of our tower orders the records byte-identically to the fix (ascending left, type-0 interleaved). Fix: sort each floor's unit records by ascending left before writing (`tdtEncoder.ts`), non-finite left coalesced to 0. Golden test `emits a wide floor's records in ascending left order, type-0 gap interleaved (gh-318)` + a non-finite-guard determinism test in `src/tests/integration/tdtExport.integration.test.ts`; canon note in `docs/canon/tdt-format.md` §4. Harness gained a `save` mode (drives File>Save As) for the round-trip. Original: **Gated: deep RE + harness iteration, unproven cause.** ... |

(b) Full save-in-game round-trip exercised via the new harness `save` mode: our export -> load in the real game -> File>Save As (a genuine 253 KB game-written `.TDT`) -> import back into our engine preserves all 1910 rooms (metro, 350 parking stalls, 655 offices, 447 condos, hotels, cinema, recycling). (c) **office/condo status + byte 6 + byte 17** are LIVE/derived state the game rebuilds on load (occupancy is approximate by design; byte 17 is nonzero ONLY for offices/condos and 0 for every other kind, so it tracks the crowd, not persisted data); our zero/`1` writes are benign, same posture as the zero-filled people block. (d) "**hotel days-dirty**" is MOOT: byte 17 is literally `0` for hotels in real saves, so there is nothing to preserve. Net: gap (3) is documented-lossy-by-design or already-working, not a fixable round-trip bug; making a real save byte-identical would mean preserving live state the game regenerates (engine pollution) - an explicit non-goal unless the owner wants it. The elevator built-payload + routing-tail OVER-emission (our export is ~151 KB larger than the game's own re-save but LOADS/renders fine) is tracked in [[tdt-export-routing-tail]] (#310) as a size/fidelity nicety, not a correctness bug. Canon §4 byte-17 note corrected to record the offices/condos nonzero finding. |
| 2026-07-13 | tdt-elevator-many-shafts | — | SimTower parity | bug | P2 | high | — | resolved | **FIXED 2026-07-13 (v1.26.4).** ROOT CAUSE (harness-confirmed against the real 1994 game): the built-shaft payload was sized `3140 + serviced*324 + cars*348`, but the appended 348-byte car block is **cars-INDEPENDENT** (one per shaft, not one per car). The `* cars` was only ever validated on 1-car saves (my_tower and every native reference save we have is cars=1), so it silently over-sized every multi-car shaft. On the owner's "six seven" (shafts up to 8 cars) the retail game desynced the whole elevator table after the first multi-car shaft: ONLY ONE ELEVATOR RENDERED, and the parking/basement block after the table mis-read too (the "missing basement" symptom). FIX: `tdtExport.ts` + `tdtFormat.ts` (parser skip) + `tdtBuilder` fixture now write `3140 + serviced*324 + 348` (car count still truthful in the header). PROVEN: forcing the fixed size in a hand-edited six-seven export made all shafts render in Wine; a clean re-export renders all 11 (old tower) / 15 (sixseven_3) shafts down to B1. Canon doc corrected (docs/canon/tdt-format.md). NOTE: a separate residual is a floors-12/13 right-side sky gap in the real-game render that the export data does NOT explain (verified data-complete 3 ways: all tiles, floor header extents == records, and room-tile-by-type all match the source vctower, basement included); candidate is the zero-filled per-floor index/remap table (tdtExport.ts:647), but that has always been zero and my_tower renders fully, so it is unproven - own follow-up if it reproduces on a clean export. Original investigation notes: byte diff cross-checked against TS parseTdtBinary (agree exactly); overlap theory refuted (0 collisions); my_tower's 3 spread shafts always rendered. DEFERS from the 2026-07-13 gds-code-review (both adversarial layers): (a) rename `TDT_ELEVATOR_PER_CAR_SIZE` -> `TDT_ELEVATOR_CAR_BLOCK_SIZE` (name says 'per car' but the block is once-per-shaft, inviting a future `* cars` revert); mitigated by a clarifying doc-comment at the definition AND a new guard test (`tdtExport.test.ts` 'built-shaft payload size is car-count INDEPENDENT') that fails on any revert - so the rename is cosmetic follow-up. (b) `tools/simtower/docker/entrypoint.sh` MAXIMIZE/ZOOM_CLICKS click coords are hardcoded to the default window and do NOT scale with a wide `SCREEN` (SCREEN is validated to WxH and the coords are hardcoded to the default window, both harmless for an opt-in diagnostic). |

| 2026-07-08 | lobby-height | — | Classic parity | feature-request | P1 | — | — | done | **Closed on GitHub (completed, #308, 2026-11-13); row trued up by the 2026-11-15 backlog audit.** **Post-3.0 (backlog triage 2026-10-28): the .TDT codec moves into the Rust crate in 3.x (replan decision 3, Oct 27), so the fidelity work is re-scoped there.** **Gated: tier-2, needs a spec. Spec seed: gdd-classic-modern-pricing-roadmap-2026-07-08 §4.** Buildable 1-3 story ground lobby (TDT header `lobbyHeight` byte 1-3; tdt-format.md §1). Today lobbies are a single-story transit layer (`facilities.ts`); the grand lobby is purely cosmetic dressing (PARITY:80). The audit ranked this the most VISIBLE missing thing in the game (the iconic tall grand lobby is a real buildable structural choice in the original). M-L; touches structure/placement/render + a save field. The importer's header parse doesn't consume `lobbyHeight` yet either. **EXTENDED (owner observation 2026-07-08, real mobile tower via harness): this is NOT just the ground lobby. SKY lobbies (floors 15/30/45/...) also have 1-3 story heights in the real 1994 game; we model every lobby (ground AND sky) as single-story (`facilities.ts`; `Tower.ts:16` "ground floor + every 15th"). So importing a real tower whose sky/ground lobbies are 2-3 stories LOSES that height (they come in single-story), and export writes `lobbyHeight` 0/1 only (`tdtExport.ts` setHdrU16 0x1c). This is a Classic parity gap (multi-story lobbies are canon structural choices, the iconic grand lobby), and it degrades the TDT round-trip fidelity we are otherwise fixing. Resolve in CLASSIC first (canon 1-3 story ground + sky lobbies, round-trip the height byte + the sky-lobby heights); optionally richer in Modern. Blocks full round-trip fidelity for towers with tall lobbies (e.g. the owner's mobile save). CONFIRMED CANON + the exact mechanic (SimTower Wiki, owner 2026-07-08): multi-level lobbies are a hidden input, hold `Ctrl` (2-level) or `Shift`+`Ctrl` (3-level) when FIRST placing a lobby (ground OR sky, so the sky-lobby extension holds). Effects to model: it changes the stairs/escalator appearance, elevators CANNOT stop on the extra lobby floors, and the floor number does NOT appear in the shaft on those floors. Lobby price (corrected 2026-11-04 from the official Windows Help and Read Me, which outrank the wiki): $5,000 per section, and the lobby cursor places four sections at once where it can ($20,000 per placement); a 2-story lobby costs $40,000 per placement and a 3-story one $60,000; capacity infinite; cannot be placed underground; cannot be deleted. HEIGHT RULE (owner 2026-11-04, to confirm on the PC; plan in the #308 comment): every lobby section on a floor shares one height, set by that floor's first lobby placement, while lobbies on different floors may differ. Where a sky lobby's height is stored in the .TDT is unknown (the header field covers the ground lobby only). So `lobbyHeight` 1-3 is player-controlled and the round-trip should carry it. BUILD: the Ctrl/Shift+Ctrl placement modifier + the no-stop / no-floor-number render+dispatch consequences + round-trip the height on TDT import/export. Owner will generate a multi-story-lobby real save (headless Ctrl-place test is a follow-up) as a fixture.** |

| 2026-07-08 | auto-floor-build | — | SimTower parity | feature-request | P3 | — | — | done | **Both behaviors SHIPPED (behavior 2 v1.15.0, behavior 1 v1.25.0).** Owner observation 2026-07-08 playing the real game via the harness. Two related 1994 QOL behaviors: (1) **extending an elevator past the built structure auto-creates the floor behind it** (no separate floor-build step); (2) **floors auto-fill the empty gap between two modules** placed with space between them. **(2) DONE (v1.15.0):** placing a room (or lobby) auto-fills the horizontal gap to its nearest same-substrate neighbor on each of its own stories, a room with plain floor and a lobby with lobby tiles; charged for the bridge tiles, blocked when the whole run is unaffordable. **(1) DONE (v1.25.0, owner-requested):** `Tower.resizeTransport` auto-lays plain floor behind the shaft on every newly-served floor (up OR down into the basement), filling every empty column of the shaft footprint (partial floors are completed too) in support order via `Tower.layShaftFloors` (bottom-up for a grow, top-down for a basement, retry-loop drained), and rolls the batch back and refuses only when a tile can never be supported (a genuinely floating column) so the shaft never floats. **Also in v1.25.0: the plain FLOOR tool now bridges** to a neighboring floor exactly like a room/lobby (the earlier "floor tool never bridges" carve-out is gone; owner asked for floor-between-tiles fill), with the ground-floor bridge rescue extended to floors and the per-tile floor charge. Both mode-agnostic. **Sky-lobby floors (15/30/45…) are exempt from the auto-floor:** extending a shaft through an UNBUILT sky-lobby story refuses ("Build the sky lobby on floor N first, then extend through it.") rather than laying plain floor (which would pollute the concourse and block the player's later lobby) or auto-committing a permanent lobby the player never placed; a sky-lobby floor that already carries its lobby passes through normally. Reviewed via `/gds-code-review` (Blind + Edge Case hunters) plus Copilot + Codex rounds: partial-floor gap fixed pre-merge, basement bridge rescue, shared retry-loop DRY, unified overlap message, sky-lobby exemption; see the `auto-floor-extend-charge` deferral below for the one design note. |
| 2026-07-13 | auto-floor-extend-charge | — | SimTower parity | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | superseded | **CLOSED NOT PLANNED 2026-10-28 (owner triage; #346 closed).** Watch-only item retired; kept for the record. **Watch-only, by design. From the v1.25.0 `auto-floor-build` (1) review (gds-code-review, Blind Hunter).** The elevator-extend auto-floor (behavior 1) folds the auto-created floor tiles into the existing per-floor extend charge (`ECON.transportFloorCost`, $5,000/shaft-floor) rather than billing `floor.cost` per tile on top, so a shaft-floor is one priced action (matching the 1994 "no separate floor-build step"). This is deliberately asymmetric with the FLOOR-tool bridge, which charges `floor.cost` per gap tile. It is not an exploit: extending a shaft is strictly MORE expensive per floor than laying the tiles by hand ($5,000/floor vs $500/tile), so nobody floors a tower via elevators to save money. Itemizing the floor charge cleanly (without a negative-money edge in the budget-clamped `extendBill` drag path, which knows only a single flat per-floor cost and can't tell sky floors from built ones) is disproportionate to the ~$500/tile amounts. If the owner wants the extend to itemize its floor cost, add a non-mutating `Tower` count of the tiles a resize would create, fold it into `extendBill`'s per-step budget, and charge it in the engine's extend command (`extendTransport` / `extend_transport`), which owns the extend charge since #914. |
| 2026-07-09 | auto-floor-bridge-distance | — | SimTower parity | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | superseded | **CLOSED NOT PLANNED 2026-10-28 (owner triage; #347 closed).** Watch-only item retired; kept for the record. **Watch-only, by design. From the v1.15.0 `auto-floor-build` (2) review (gds-code-review, Acceptance Auditor + Edge Case Hunter).** `Tower.bridgeFillPlan` bridges to the *nearest* same-substrate structure with no maximum-gap cap and scans out to the lot edge. Two linked notes: (a) a distant nearest-neighbor auto-fills (and charges for) a long floor run, and can block an otherwise-affordable module with only a generic "Not enough money." toast; (b) when no neighbor exists on a story, the hover-preview scan runs to the lot edge each frame (bounded, cheap `Map.get`s, ~2xGRID.width per story, but at hover cadence). Kept nearest-neighbor unbounded because that matches the owner's stated intent ("fill the gap between them") and is pinned by a far-gap test. If the far-gap fill reads as surprising, add a max-gap cap (and a bridge-specific refusal message); if the hover scan ever shows up in a profile, short-circuit when the first column out is empty on both sides. **Added 2026-10-21 (Hot-Path Auditor record of facade slice 2, #1209):** the plan also runs three times per structural build (`canBuild`, `build`'s quoted bridge, `fillBridge`), and a floor or lobby drag builds per dragged tile on every pointer move, so a drag into open floor costs up to about 3,400 visits per move and about 34k on a fast flick; computing the plan once per build and passing it along cuts that to one scan with no change to charges (details on #347). |

| 2026-07-08 | tdt-trailing-structure-layout | — | SimTower parity | bug | P2 | high | — | resolved | **RESOLVED (trued up 2026-07-15 during the issue-mirror pass; the notes below already declared both halves resolved while the status cell lagged at open). The live remainder is tracked on its own row, [[tdt-export-routing-tail]] (GH #310); issue #309 was opened from the stale status and is closed as completed.** **EPIC. CONFIRMED against the real 1994 game (Wine harness, tools/simtower/). Affects BOTH import and export -- one root cause.** Our model of the `.TDT` trailing structures (everything after the floor map: §6 people, §7 retail, §8 elevators+stairs, §9 finance, §10 parking, §11 lobby/reachability table) does NOT match the real game's actual layout. Symptoms: (a) EXPORT -- our `.TDT` crashes real SimTower on load (16-bit page fault 0x0799, a buffer overrun) while the game's own saves load fine; our empty export is 39,514 B vs the game's 65,112 (~25.6 KB short of trailing tables), so the game reads past our too-small tables. (b) IMPORT -- our own import report says *"the elevator table doesn't match the documented layout, so elevators were rebuilt from the floor layout and the save's stairways couldn't be read"*: we FALL BACK to synthesizing 1 elevator from the floors, LOSING the real elevators + all stairs/escalators (visible in the round-trip: a real my_tower with multiple shafts imports as 1). RE of the real empty save (TSTSAVE.TDT) shows the real order/offsets differ from our canon: e.g. a ~8.2 KB structure sits between the floor map end (0x5D20) and the retail table (real retail at 0x7D24, ours right after the floor map), and the §11 lobby table (528x6) is emitted not at all. FIX (epic, spec-first): re-derive the trailing layout from the real saves (empty TSTSAVE.TDT + my_tower.TDT which has elevators+stairs) as ground truth, correct `docs/canon/tdt-format.md` §6-11, then fix `tdtFormat.ts`/`tdtImport.ts` (parse the real elevator/stair tables instead of falling back) AND `tdtExport.ts` (emit every table at the real offset+size). GUARDS: importer reads the real my_tower's elevators+stairs (no fallback); exporter byte-length + structure offsets match TSTSAVE.TDT; re-verify load in the real game (no 0x0799). NOTE: header + floor map + room import are already CORRECT (rooms/funds/star/date faithful, 0 hard warnings); this is trailing-tables only. Neither symptom is player-shipping-critical today (importer degrades gracefully; export-to-1994 is niche), which is why it's P2 not P1. **RESOLVED both halves. IMPORT half fixed v1.13.0 (real elevator/stair tables parsed, no synthesis fallback). EXPORT half fixed v1.14.0: a full my_tower (2★, offices/hotels/condos/fast food/3 shafts/6 stairs) now LOADS AND PLAYS in the real 1994 game (Wine harness) with no 0x0799 crash. Root causes were, in order: (1) file too short -- the game reads a fixed trailing routing region after the stairs table; emit it 0xFF-filled to TDT_ROUTING_TAIL_SIZE (0xFF = empty-slot sentinel; zero-fill invents a phantom population); (2) a POPULATED tower with people count 0 faults -- write the resident/worker census with that many zeroed records (empty tower stays 0); (3) built shafts with a zero schedule block load with NO CARS (people trapped) -- emit the game's default block TDT_ELEVATOR_SCHEDULE_DEFAULT (01x14 05x14 00x28); (4) the saved view opened on the sky -- write the New Tower view-scroll default (header 0x26/0x28) so it opens on the ground lobby. Isolation done by blanking each structure in the game's OWN save (index maps proved NOT required; 0xFF tail + zeroed people/car records all load). Guarded by src/tests/tdtExport.test.ts. Remaining: see `tdt-export-routing-tail`. |
| 2026-07-08 | tdt-export-routing-tail | #73 | SimTower parity | task | P3 | med | — | open | **Post-3.0 (backlog triage 2026-10-28): the .TDT codec moves into the Rust crate in 3.x (replan decision 3, Oct 27), so the fidelity work is re-scoped there.** **TAIL EXTENT MEASURED ON A GAME-WRITTEN 4★ RE-SAVE (2026-08-04): the game does NOT require a fixed 25,600-byte region.** Its own re-save of the owner's 4★ tower (the DEGRADED 8-shaft load, so this bounds the extent only for that file's own content) carries only **5,672 bytes after the elevator table, all zeros** (no recognizable finance values, parking count, or stairs records), and the game re-opens that file cleanly. So the extent the game reads is a function of tower content with a small floor, the fixed `0xFF` over-emit stays on the safe side of every measured case, and the long-standing NEEDS (3★/4★ game-written saves) is retired: re-saves can now be minted at will via `File > Save` (no `COMMDLG.DLL` needed when the tower was loaded from a path). Remaining: 5★/6★ (Tower level) unvalidated, and the exact size law is still un-RE'd (fidelity nicety, not a correctness need). **RE-MEASURE THE SIZE CLAIM (2026-07-31).** The "the game's own re-save is much smaller (253 KB vs our 404 KB), so our elevator payload region is larger than the game writes" comparison below was taken while our exporter was sizing each built shaft's payload by SERVICED floors. That under-ran every shaft with skip floors, and the retail game consequently DROPPED most of the tower's shafts on load (measured 2026-07-31: a 4-shaft test tower rendered 2), so the game re-saved a tower that had far fewer elevators than the one we exported. The 151 KB delta is therefore not evidence that the game writes a smaller per-shaft payload; that inference has to be redone against a re-save made from a file the game reads completely. The fixed `0xFF` tail half of the delta still stands. Our export of the same tower is now 453 KB (up from 399 KB) because the corrected spanned-floor stride emits the entries the game expects. **4★ CONFIRMATION 2026-07-19 (Wine harness): the fixed `0x6400` tail is a SAFE upper bound well past 2★.** A dense 4★ tower (owner's `sixseven_14`: 91 floors, ~1910 rooms, 45 transports, metro, sky lobbies) LOADS AND RENDERS fully with our fixed-size tail, so over-emitting holds at 4★ and there is no 0x0799 crash at scale. The game's OWN re-save of that tower is much smaller (253 KB vs our 404 KB) and carries NO trailing `0xFF` block at all (it ends in zeros); the 151 KB delta is the fixed `0xFF` tail (25,600 bytes) plus a larger elevator built-payload region than the game writes. So matching the game's exact (smaller, variable) tail is now a FIDELITY/size nicety, not a correctness need: over-emit is confirmed safe. Downgrade the crash risk. RE'ing the exact tail-size law + shrinking the elevator payload to the game's is a follow-up (deep RE; the current oversized file is harness-confirmed to load). Original: **Follow-up to the now-resolved `tdt-trailing-structure-layout` export half (v1.14.0).** The exporter reaches the game's expected file length by emitting a FIXED-size `0xFF` routing tail (`TDT_ROUTING_TAIL_SIZE` = 0x6400), validated for Classic towers up to 2★ (the only real saves on hand: empty TSTSAVE.TDT + a 2★ my_tower). The real game's trailing region is not a single fixed size across saves, so a much bigger 3★+ tower (more elevators/floors, sky lobbies, big crowds, cinemas/cathedral) could read past the fixed tail and 0x0799 again. Over-emitting is safe (the game ignores trailing slack); under-emitting is the crash. TODO once higher-star real saves are available: pin the tail SIZE as a function of tower content (or confirm a safe upper bound) and replace the fixed constant. Also refine the people census: our count is low (77 for a tower the game runs at ~291) because the IMPORT under-captures office/condo occupancy (see `tdt-import-population-seed`) and commercial customers aren't counted (see `population-census-parity`); the export faithfully mirrors whatever we import, so this rides those two items. NEEDS: a few 3★/4★ saves with varied transport (multiple/express/service elevators, escalators, sky lobbies, parking) to RE the tail-size law. |
| 2026-07-08 | classic-calendar-parity | — | Classic/Modern split | feature-request | P1 | med | — | done | **DONE (trued up 2026-07-15 during the issue-mirror pass): PR #170 (v1.15.0) merged with the harness weekend-phase validation in the PR body; the impl-review status was stale.** **IMPLEMENTED 2026-07-08 (v1.15.0), pending gds-review + owner harness weekend-phase validation. New `src/engine/calendar.ts` (Calendar model: CANON 3/3/12 + REAL_WORLD 7/90/360, resolveCalendar/coerceCalendarKind); Clock holds a Calendar (default real-world so bare `new Clock()` is unchanged) and derives dayOfWeek/isWeekend/quarter/year/formatRetroDate from it; Simulation resolves the calendar from mode + a new persisted `modernCalendar` toggle and builds the clock with it; onDay maintenance rides `calendar.maintPeriodDays` (killed `day/30`); EconomySystem collectRent/payMaintenance income-invariant rescale (canon rent 1/30, maint 1/10; real-world factor exactly 1 = byte-identical); condoRelocation chance scaled by period; maybeSanta calendar-aware (real-world byte-identical); New-Tower Modern calendar toggle (UI.ts/main.ts/saveLoad.ts); no SAVE_VERSION bump (additive field, existing Classic saves SHOULD adopt canon = the fix). Guarded by src/tests/calendar.test.ts (20) + faqComplete fixups. Gates green (typecheck/lint/951 tests/build). gds-code-review DONE (3 layers; 7 patched: REAL_WORLD-linked divisors, lunch-gate + dayName calendar-aware, real-world maintenance wording restored, weekend-phase comment fix, added maint-invariance/cadence tests, doc reconcile; 2 deferred: 999-yr roll + non-trailing weekend phase; 2 dismissed). REMAINING before merge: (1) owner harness weekend-PHASE validation (which canon day-slot the real game treats as weekend for a known currentDay -- currently pinned to the trailing slot with a documented TODO in calendar.ts CANON; CONTINGENT FOLLOW-UP if the game's weekend is a NON-trailing slot: the trailing-only `weekendDays`-count model can't express it, so it would need an explicit weekend-phase offset on the Calendar + Clock arithmetic -- gds-review Auditor flag); (3) optional maintenance-cadence confirmation vs the Finance window. Date round-trip already proven (day 1280 -> Year 107). SPEC: `gdd-/arch-classic-calendar-parity-2026-07-08.md`. --- ORIGINAL SPEC NOTE 2026-07-08: `gdd-classic-calendar-parity-2026-07-08.md` + `arch-classic-calendar-parity-2026-07-08.md`. Party VERDICT: full canon calendar for Classic (display AND cadence); economy kept balanced by INCOME-INVARIANT RESCALE (per-collection amount x periodDays/oldPeriodDays, so money-per-in-game-day is unchanged; canon rent = rentOf/30 every 3 days) rather than canon amounts (blocked -- the 1994 rent table is in the un-OCR'd German manual); ONE mode-resolved `Calendar` where Modern real-world = today's constants EXACTLY (factor 1.0, byte-identical, the contained-risk safety net); kill the incoherent `day/30` maintenance month (longer than a canon year) -> canon per-quarter; rescale any per-year EventSystem cadence; `modernCalendar` save field + version decision; three harness validations gate merge (weekend phase, maintenance cadence, date round-trip). The change is ATOMIC: `onDay()` collects rent on `clock.quarter` change, so flipping the quarter without the rescale = 30x income bug; the mode gate is what makes atomic safe. SEQUENCING (owner 2026-07-08): designated FAST-FOLLOW immediately after the TDT export fix -- it is tightly TDT-coupled (the save's date is part of the round-trip) -- and AHEAD of the population-census work. Scope lever: consider decoupling the cheap DATE DISPLAY (what TDT needs) from the risky ECONOMIC QUARTER pacing (rent timing) if the full change is too heavy for a fast-follow. EPIC, spec-first. Revisits the earlier "3-day-week ratified-out" note on the elevator-scheduling row.** Adopt SimTower's real calendar for CLASSIC: a week = 3 days (2 weekday + 1 weekend), a quarter = 1 week (3 days), a year = 4 quarters = **12 days** (canon: `docs/canon/tdt-format.md` §3; day counter rolls at 11,987 = 999 years). Today our `Clock` (`src/engine/Clock.ts`) uses a 7-day week / 90-day quarter / 360-day year, a DELIBERATE divergence recorded in PARITY.md; the retro WD/WE date display already exists. Plan: put week/quarter/year lengths behind `GameRules`/`GameMode` so **Classic = the 12-day canon calendar** and **Modern = a startup TOGGLE (classic-short vs real-world-length)**. Payoff: makes `.TDT` date import/export EXACT (`currentDay` 56 -> "5th Year/3Q/WE", which our 360-day calendar currently renders as "1st Year"); closes a named parity gap. RISK / why it's an epic not a quick fix: the calendar length drives QUARTERLY RENT COLLECTION (a 3-day quarter collects rent every 3 days -- a real economic-pacing change), weekend crowd/office-closed behavior, milestones, and VIP/`completeAt`/`vacate` timers; needs a save-version gate so Classic replays stay deterministic, plus its own gdd/arch spec and tests. Interim (until this lands): TDT import keeps the honest report note that the original's date does not map 1:1 to our calendar. Supersedes the "3-day-week/12-day-calendar stays ratified-out" line on the `elevator-scheduling` row. |
