| 2026-10-08 | engine-rs-hand-edited-save-fidelity | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **Done (2026-10-21, B6; issue #858 closes on merge): every item below now loads the same in both engines, except a number literal past the double range (`1e400`), proposed as EX-3 in `release-3.0-exception-register.md`. The lone-surrogate item already matched: both engines read save text through the same repair (`parseSaveJson` and `parse_save_json`, #936), and `conformanceWasmVctower.integration.test.ts` pins it.** The TypeScript loader narrows each mistyped field to the type the game writes it as, after a per-field check of the documented format and every writer in the history (the table in `spec-gh-858-856-loader-fidelity.md`); the Rust loader clamps its saved integers at 2^53 - 1 where it saturated at the edge of `i64`, caps `minutes` there, and refuses a `null` save as the TypeScript does. The two `knownDivergence` loader cases are plain cases now, beside 39 new ones for #858 and 3 for #856; every other case keeps its hash. The v1 to v5 checks and the reflow also read structure geometry as numbers in both engines, skip or leave out a room at a NaN column or with a floor, column or width at or past 2^52, cap a tile's width at the lot's width and pave only lot columns; both floor a fractional v1 `nextId` (as v5 to v6 already did) and read a pending message that is not a string as none. A fractional `vipVisitDay`, and one past 2^63, already matched (both engines keep the double). Three forged shapes outside this list (an object with its own `toString` in a coerced field, more than 2^24 v1 tile keys, a huge top-level string or array), with two load passes only forged saves make superlinear, are `forged-save-loader-edges`, #1282. If the owner rejects EX-3 at the #945 sign-off, the `1e400` work goes back on its own row and issue. Original row: The Rust loader matches the TypeScript `deserialize` on every save the engine writes (the referee's three fixtures and the round trips prove it), but a hand-edited save can still read differently: a fractional `occupants`, `carDir`, `load`, `blockbusters`, `lastSantaYear` or `vipVisitDay` is kept as a double by the TypeScript and narrowed to an integer by the Rust (a `vipVisitDay` past 2^63 saturates to `i64::MAX`); a present `evaluatedTower` or `builtWeddingHall` that is not a boolean, or a `towerName` that is not a string, is kept verbatim by the TypeScript and narrowed to `false` or `""` by the Rust (a truthy non-boolean `vipFavorable` likewise reads as false); a `1e400` literal parses as Infinity in JavaScript and fails the Rust parse at the JSON level, before any field coercion runs, so closing it needs a lenient number reader rather than a field rule; a non-string `state` on a pre-mode condo skips the legacy price backfill; a lone-surrogate escape in a log line fails the whole Rust parse where TypeScript truncates the entry; and the v1 to v2 reflow trusts a save's `width` as the TypeScript does, so an absurd width runs the same unbounded loop. None of these inputs can come from the game's own writer. Close by giving the loader JavaScript number semantics on those fields. Found by the Blind Hunter and Edge Case Hunter layers on #857 (`story-engine-rust-port`); the `skipFloors`, `completeAt` and reflow-overflow items it first listed were fixed by the loader table in #861, which carries the remaining ones as `knownDivergence` cases. **Port audit 2026-10-09 (serialization slice) adds:** the Rust loader types what the TypeScript keeps raw, so a forged value of the wrong type loads differently in `evaluatedTower`, `vipFavorable`, `builtWeddingHall` and `towerName` (truthiness and re-save of a non-boolean or non-string), `carDir` fractions, the transport `load` field (absent in TypeScript when the save lacks it, `0` here) and unknown transport keys (re-saved by the TypeScript spread, dropped here), `lastVipNagDay` beyond i64, the legacy condo rent backfill when `state` is not a string, v1 reflow with a fractional `nextId` or non-number geometry, a top-level `null` save (a TypeError there, an empty tower here), `Clock.day` above about 1.3e22 minutes, and `pending.message` of a non-string (prose). Fixed with the audit: `events` of a truthy non-object resets the event state as the TypeScript does, a fractional `lastSantaYear`, fractional blockbuster ids and a fractional `vipVisitDay` are kept, `parseInt` skips the byte order mark; the first four are loader cases and the byte order mark is a Rust unit test (the loader table's base saves carry no `appVersion`). Added by the dual-run review (2026-10-09): `vipVisitDay` as a string or boolean (TypeScript keeps it through `?? -1` and compares coerced; the Rust loader reads it as no visit). |
| 2026-10-21 | forged-save-loader-edges | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | superseded | **#1282: CLOSED NOT PLANNED 2026-11-12 (owner-approved open-issue audit): obsolete: the TypeScript engine was removed in #1956, so the code it describes is gone (removed, not fixed). The Rust loader already loads these shapes.** **Ready (deferred from the B6 `/gds-code-review` round 2, Edge Case Hunter and Acceptance Auditor, and the independent review of #1327).** Three forged save shapes the TypeScript loader refuses or loads slowly where the Rust loader loads them, and two load passes that only forged saves make superlinear; none can come from a writer the game has shipped. (1) A JSON object with its own `toString` or `valueOf` key in a field the loader coerces (`Number()`, `String()`, a template string or `>>>`: the v1 to v5 checks and reflow in `v1tov2.ts`, the `seed` passed to the `Simulation` constructor) throws "Cannot convert object to primitive value" in TypeScript, where Rust reads NaN; so do array-wrapped shapes (`[{"toString":1}]`, `[[{"toString":1}]]`) and an object with both keys (`{"toString":"x","valueOf":"y"}`) in `seed`, a v1 room `x` or a v1 tile `floor`. Fix: one guarded number read that takes a non-primitive as NaN, at every coercion of a save value, after an audit of the loader. (2) A v1 to v5 save whose structure tiles cover more than 2^24 distinct floor:column keys (about 45,000 lot-wide tiles, 2.7 MB) throws "Set maximum size exceeded" in TypeScript's `floatingStructureCount` and the reflow's sets; Rust builds about 16.9M string keys (slow, memory-heavy, may run out of memory under WASM). Fix: bound the keys in both engines (a tile off the lot or outside the floor range counts as nothing), or per-floor sets. (3) A multi-megabyte top-level JSON string or array: TypeScript's `migrateSave` spreads one key per element (about 3 s per 4M characters) before loading the same empty tower Rust loads at once. Fix: read a top-level string or array as `{}` before the spread. (4) Two load passes the Hot-Path Auditor found superlinear only on forged saves: Rust dedupes saved `milestones` and `blockbusters` by linear scan (TypeScript uses Sets; already #1252 H15, which carries the fix), and the v1 reflow's parking pass filters every room once per parking floor in both engines. Rust side of (4) is done (#1458, 2026-10-29: both passes group or keep a set in one pass); the TypeScript reflow's per-floor filter is left. The v5 party-hall pass searched every floor and column again for each hall that found no room (about 10 s for 10,000 halls in a debug build); Rust now remembers a failed width until a hall is placed (#1458). Two follow-ups from the #1458 independent review: keep a smallest-failed-width memo that placements never clear (a failed width stays failed for every wider hall), and give the parking timing test an absolute bound beside its ratio. Context: `spec-gh-858-856-loader-fidelity.md` (D6). |

| 2026-10-10 | charge-command-input-trust | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **DONE (story `charge-input-trust`, PR #989, closes #923 on merge).** Both engines refuse a malformed charge command (an id, floor or mark that is not a whole number, an unknown `end` or `method`) with one reason, `"That request is not valid."`, before anything moves; the WASM binding reads each argument raw (a number only from a whole JS number, an enum only from the exact JS string, a mark only as strict JSON text with integer fields) instead of coercing, truncating to `i32` or throwing, and a raw-binding test pins every malformed case against the TypeScript answer and the intentionally unsupported mark forms (listed in the story) as binding refusals. The narrow-mark billing hole was already closed by #914 (the mark widens to the current span) and is now pinned by a test. The relay ops stay confined by `towerRelayOps.guard.test.ts` (owner decision, 2026-10-13: the WASM host relays through the mirror in production, so they cannot be compiled out). The engine-held gesture mark moved to its own row, `engine-held-extend-mark` (#990). Original note: **From the `/gds-code-review` of the engine-owned charges (#914, story-engine-owned-charges).** The charge commands trust their arguments like every relayed command: the TypeScript engine takes a NaN or fractional `targetFloor` or high-water mark, reads any `end` other than `"up"` as `"down"` and any removal `method`, while the Rust binding parses them strictly (`i32` truncation, `parse_hwm` / `parse_extend_end` errors), so a malformed relayed command errors in the dual-run shadow rather than diverging cleanly. The web UI never sends these values; a second frontend could. Separately, `extendTransport` bills against the caller's high-water mark (widened to the current span, never bounded by what the gesture paid), so an inflated mark gets free floors: the same trust the free raw `resizeTransport` already gives, so no new hole; the web editor binds its mark to the sim it was taken on. The dual-run mirror normalizes `id` and `targetFloor` with `n()` but copies the mark's `bottom` and `top` raw, so a fractional mark reaches the binding's `parse_hwm` and errors there (second review of the #903 seam, 2026-10-11). The silent relay ops `towerSetCars` and `towerResizeTransport` (#906, 2026-10-13) sit on the same production binding: a guard test keeps every caller but the dual-run relay off them, and the gate should confine them to the relay (or a debug/conformance build) outright. Fix: one boundary validation in both engines (finite whole numbers, known enums), the mirror normalizing the mark like its other fields, and consider an engine-held gesture mark. |

The caller-supplied mark is gone from both engines, the binding, the mirror and the scenario ops, so an inflated mark buys nothing. `EditorActions` opens and closes the gesture instead of holding a mark. The conformance scenario moved to `beginExtend`/`endExtend` ops and two new checkpoints, `dragged` and `dragged down`, pin each drag's money and a billed regrow after each `endExtend` (dropping any one begin or end op moves a hash). A shadow or WASM host started mid-drag takes the open gesture over with the boundary markers. Fix shape: the engine holds the gesture mark in both engines (for example `beginExtend(id)`, `extendTransport` billing against the held mark, `endExtend()`; a tower swap, load or new gesture drops it), relayed by the dual-run mirror and the binding; `EditorActions` moves onto it and drops its caller-held `hwm`; a scenario op pins a wiggling drag billed once; the mark stays out of the save and the hashed views. `/gds-code-review`. |
| 2026-10-14 | extend-drag-lifecycle | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P1 | low | — | done | **DONE (closes #1014 and #824 on merge): a canvas press ends as a cancel on a release off the canvas, a window blur, a mouse back with its button up, a fresh first press, or a change of the selected shaft (`src/render/excalibur/pressLifecycle.ts`, `src/game/lostRelease.ts`).** **Ready (deferred from the round-2 `/gds-code-review` of #990, PR #1011).** An extend-arrow drag ends only on the canvas `onExtendEnd` (pointer-up or a pinch start). A swallowed pointer-up leaves the gesture open (and it is not rare: Excalibur listens on the canvas only and the game sets no pointer capture, so a mouse released over the inspector panel never ends the drag), so the next one-floor press or arrow click bills against that drag's mark (already-paid floors regrow free and no new undo step: this half never overcharges). The arrow click and the selection paths predate #990; the press half is new with it (on main the button billed from the span, so it could bill a paid floor twice). A selection that clears or changes while the pointer is held lets the drag carry on and begin a fresh gesture from the span, recapturing undo over the pending step, so already-paid floors can bill again and Undo lands mid-drag. Fix shape: end the canvas drag (`arrowDrag`) with the editor's gesture and commit the pending undo on a selection change or clear, close any open gesture on a press or click that arrives with no pointer down (so the press bills like any step after a closed drag; the editor coverage test that pins today's free regrow changes with it), and test through the input path. A round-1 attempt that closed only the editor's gesture (in `clearSelection` and the one-floor button) was reverted because the held drag then re-billed. The same lost release leaves a tool press's undo gesture open (widened from the round-2 review of #1789): a build or bulldoze drag released off the canvas, or cut off by a lost pointer capture or a window blur, keeps its gesture until the next press, so the Undo button stays gray for its edits meanwhile; Ctrl+Z and a click on the gray Undo still close and undo it (#1789). Worse, `pointerMove` never checks the button state, so a mouse that comes back over the canvas with the button up keeps painting, bulldozing or sizing a shaft, and that spends money (round-3 review of #1789). Fix shape for both halves: end a canvas press on a release anywhere on the page, a lost capture or a blur, as a cancel (`onActionCancel`), and test through the input path. `/gds-code-review`. |

The relayed tick and `adoptSim`'s undo history are ruled out; the likely cause is deserialized units taking different hidden-class shapes from `tower.place`'d ones, to confirm with a profile. Every returning player runs on a loaded tower, and the committed perf baseline measures an in-place one. Work: profile, normalize unit construction, then consider a loaded perf fixture. Also (facade slice 4 PR 4a, round 3 Hot-Path Auditor): `stats()` walks every unit, structure tiles included, though every kind it counts is a room kind, so walking `roomUnits` gives identical counts in the same order (about 46k to 5.8k visits per HUD tick) and the population can come from the same pass. Do not cache `stats()` by revision alone: the result reads tenancy, condo sales, hotel states, `noRate`, population, money, star and fires, which a revision does not track. Facade slice 4's 4e moves the HUD strip onto the `towerStats` query and must not carry the whole-unit walk with it. Gate: `stats()` on a loaded tower within 10% of the in-place one. `/gds-code-review`. **Update (2026-10-27):** the walk part is done by `story-hud-stats-rooms.md` (PR #1340, Refs #1051): `stats()` reads room counts kept as the rooms change (`roomCounts` in `src/engine/tower/rooms.ts`), and the HUD refresh reads it once. Each write of a counted field marks its room: the engine's through `setRoomState` and `markRoom`, the hosts' frame apply and full merge for the rooms whose fields they change, and the worker's hourly delta for every unit it rewrites. A guard pins the raw writes of those fields per file, so a new one fails until its author updates the table; the seeded run and the targeted cases hold the marks to the walk over every unit. That is the flat HUD tier HP-12 asks for between the passes that change rooms (Hot-Path Auditor F2, which the owner chose to fix on 2026-10-27). Cloud, the large tower: one HUD refresh with nothing marked 1.98 ms to under a microsecond; after a step, single-digit microseconds at the median; after the daily pass that changes about 1,440 rooms, 0.7 to 1.0 ms once (`story-hud-stats-rooms.md` Result; the live figure is the perf gate's `ui.update`, `npm run perf`). The loaded-tower gate above stays open on this row: the HUD read no longer visits a unit, so a loaded tower would pay any such cost in the one recount after a load or a rebuilt rooms list, and the gate is to be re-measured there. |

| 2026-10-09 | wasm-host-engine-failure-fallback | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P1 | medium | — | done | **Done (2026-10-21; issue #874 closes with this row). PR #1101 merged (2026-10-20, `4fa8072`; spec `spec-874-runtime-fallback.md`). P1 for 3.0 (A3 in `release-3.0-web-plan-2026-10-20.md`); PR #1092 (#902) merged (2026-10-20, `f1777e1`) with the trap mid-booking test the plan's 9.1 held the issue open for; the owner's PC restore-point run on `4fa8072` passed on 2026-10-21: 0 extra saves on all 24 rows and one hosted save per command on every rapid-edit row (`docs/performance/results/2026-10-21-4fa8072-restore-points.md`).** The main-thread host catches every throw from the engine (commands, the undo capture's save, ticks, frame syncs, drains, saves and the reads when a tower is let go) and freezes: later ticks and commands no longer reach the engine, a save answers with the newest restore point, and the frozen tower's tick does nothing. `startWasmHost` records the fault (`fallbacks`, `lastFallback`, `lastError`), reports it with a `trap-command`, `trap-tick`, `trap-undo` or `other` code and the tower's hosting stage, and latches hosting off for the page life; before the next frame, a switchable build adopts a fresh TypeScript tower from the newest restore point through the undo path, drops the undo and redo steps that would change nothing (putting back the redo steps the lost edit cleared), and toasts a log line that says the recent changes were undone (or only that the tower went back, when no relayed command changed what the player sees, or that recent changes were lost, when an undo or a redo had already replaced the faulted tower; a load or a new game gets the plain line); a wasm-only build stops through `onFatal`. The boot turns the engine save writer off on a fault. Every engine save the host already reads is a restore point (each merge's, and each hosted save, the undo capture before every edit included), so no save is added per command or tick; one more is taken only when a tick's events would leave the engine with no point behind them. A command's events are held until a point covers them and dropped on a fault, so no event of an edit a fallback takes away reaches the game. A fallback loses everything since the newest merge or hosted save: the edit under way, two edits when the earlier one had not merged (paused, or a non-structural command inside the hour) and the next capture's own save traps, any action taken without an undo capture since that point, and the game time since that point (up to an in-game hour while the clock runs). The TypeScript re-adoption is temporary compatibility code that the TypeScript-engine retirement removes. Split out: the worker host's restore (#1105, after #1068), the report of a command the engine refuses after the instance accepted it (#954), a trap while attaching (#1113), the host's own sync errors' code (#1114), the independent review's round 2 defers (#1126 to #1130), and its round 3 defers (#1147 to #1151) with the CI stall it found (#1152). The round 2 boot timeout was already done by `loadBootEngine`. `/gds-code-review` and `/bmad-code-review`. |

The refusal report it inherited from #874 has nothing left to compare.** **Ready (cutover plan review, 2026-10-13; plan story S9, spine AD-1 and AD-8).** Under `?engine=wasm` the engine alone ticks, but every command still runs on the TypeScript instance first and is relayed, so the caller gets the instance's refusal, price and result shape, and a disagreement heals silently at the next merge. (2) A coalesced structure refresh for slices 2 (#1209) and 7 (#1217, superseded by #1422) (finding F2): rebuild `viewStructure` at most once per frame, keyed on the tower revision and the engine handle (a refresh for each tile of a drag would cost 375 tiles x 0.88 ms on large). One walk could fill both caches and the grid, and the limit and the record sort could then go (or sort keys in place of records). Past the limit the read is over the per-command budget, so its deferral waits on the owner's acceptance (PR #1284). (5) Nothing counts frames that compare every room, so a host that always reads too late for the mark log would walk the rooms each frame unseen (round 3, Blind Hunter 22); a counter for the diagnostics facts (6c) and slice 9. (6) The frame fields and residents stay `pub`, so the source scan that keeps their writes in the setters goes by names and cannot see a whole unit written through a `&mut Unit` binding from another value (`*u = other`; round 4, Edge Case Hunter 3), and the same holds for the kind. Rust field privacy is relative to the module that defines the struct (`crate::tower`), so the fields cannot be private to `view/marks.rs` as such; a newtype for the six fields defined in `view/marks.rs`, with private fields and `pub(crate)` read accessors, or the setters moved into the `tower` module, would close it at compile time. `__ENGINE_PACKAGE__` is compiled into the shipped chunks and `verify-precache` checks the shell id and that the dev-only skip is not in `dist/`; the failure detail (kind, file, both ids, controlled) rides on the boot state; write-up in `/mnt/project-files/release-3.0/offline-985/unverifiable-reason.md`) is its own small PR stacked on 2a: the closed lists in `engineBoot.ts` and `scripts/engine-reliability.mjs`, the data-map row, the governed definition (outside the fault rate, a line of its own) and tests; the PostHog `fallback_reason` breakdowns gain a value. **Slice 2b-1 (the reason-specific failure screens; the story's plan splits 2b into four PRs):** `engineFailureCopy` gives mismatch (with the package ids), load-failed, timeout, unverifiable and the missing save writer a sentence each, chosen from the code, the cause and the package detail; Copy Diagnostics already rides on every stop screen. **Slice 2b still owes:** the short-body classification (a clean early end reads as `mismatch`); the stale HTTP-cache copy on an uncontrolled page; the remaining offline E2E (the 2b-4b-2 and 2b-4b-3 gaps below) and the load-cost measurement on the owner's PC. **Slice 2b-4b-3 (Repair e2e; record `review-offline-package-slice2b4b3-2026-10-27.md`):** `e2e/engineRepairDeployments.spec.ts` on the wasm-only lane: a save made online is still in its slot after a Repair and loads on the repaired engine; a Repair with the engine network cut at the server changes nothing and the next Repair works; a stop screen takes a build deployed after it appeared at once (the stop screen's update handler activates it), A's engine keys leave at activation, a second tab reloaded afterward lands on B's set, and an offline cold start runs B. Design 6.2 spec 8 and the Repair text are amended to say Repair never meets a waiting build on a stop screen. Deferred from its review: a waiting B that exists before the stop screen loads, and a Repair in flight when B activates, are untested end to end (the unit test pins that Repair leaves other-revision keys byte-identical); the cut is at the server, not at the worker. Open product question: after the whole precache is lost the shell is not re-precached, so an offline cold start after that recovery still fails; the design 6.2 spec 8 wording change is the other item for the next decision packet. **Slice 2b-4b-2 (eviction and recovery e2e; record `review-offline-package-slice2b4b2-2026-10-27.md`):** `e2e/engineEviction.spec.ts` on both wasm lanes, with `failNetwork()` added to the two-deployment server (it cuts the worker's own requests, which Playwright's offline flag does not): each evicted engine entry is fetched again with its manifest integrity and refilled under its own key, an evicted binary with the engine network cut fails with `load-failed` and stores nothing, another build's binary is never stored under the first build's key, and a lost precache with no network leaves the browser's error page and an online visit recovers the engine. Deferred from its review (spec 7 gaps): offline `load-failed` for the glue and record, and with the whole network cut; the section 5.4 result of a `load-failed` (the TypeScript fallback reason on the switchable build, the failure screen and Repair offer on wasm-only); a real second deployment (B's worker installing and waiting, then activating, during A's eviction) instead of `engineFrom("B")`; removing the whole precache while already offline; Repair after eviction and Repair with the engine fetch cut at the worker. Product question from the review: after the whole precache is lost, engine misses refill through the worker's integrity fetch, but the shell is never precached again until `sw.js` changes, so an offline cold start after that recovery still fails; decide whether the worker should re-precache itself. **Slice 2b-4b-1 (install race and first install e2e; record `review-offline-package-slice2b4b1-2026-10-27.md`):** `e2e/engineInstall.spec.ts` on both wasm lanes, with `engineFrom()` and the engine `digests` added to the two-deployment server: an update whose engine files come from another build never installs and leaves the first build's three engine entries exactly as they were (and the update installs once the files match), and a first install with another build's engine files never activates, leaves no wrong-bytes key, and a revisit after the deployment settles installs and plays offline. Deferred from its review: a worker-side `package-mismatch` that carries no package detail (the binary lacks an export the shell calls, or no `catalog`) is not latched and still retries on each adopt, so the latch would need to key on the reason as well; `status.refusal` has no reader in the diagnostics report, which shows only the last error line (surface it in the diagnostics slice). **Slice 2b-3 (cache-key rotation; record `review-offline-package-slice2b3-2026-10-27.md`):** the three engine precache entries carry the revision `<md5>-i2` (`ENGINE_REVISION_SUFFIX`, set by `engineIntegrityTransform` and compiled into the shell, which Repair uses), so an entry stored by a worker from before the integrity attribute is never found under a new worker's keys and the update fetches all three files with integrity; activation removes the old keys. The design allowed either this or a purge at first activation; the rotation needed no worker code. `e2e/offlineUpdate.spec.ts` seeds a damaged set under the plain-MD5 keys and updates to a worker with the same engine bytes (`reissueWorker` in `e2e/deployments.ts`). Cost: one re-download of the engine files (about 1.5 MB uncompressed) per player on the first update after this ships, and the old and new keys share the cache until activation. Deferred from its review: the real old worker is never run (an old-format build would be the true fixture); a suffix bump has no test of its own. **Slice 2b-2 (Repair engine files; record `review-offline-package-slice2b2-2026-10-21.md`):** the stop screen's Repair button, offered when a worker controls the page and the files did not match or did not arrive, fetches the three engine files with a parameter the precache route does not match, checks them against the compiled-in digests (the record's id is recomputed from its entries), and only then replaces the running build's three keys in place (`workbox-precache-v2-<scope>`, key `<url>?__WB_REVISION__=<md5>`), with one registration update; the revisions are compiled into the shell and `verify-precache` checks them. Deferred from its review: Repair has no timeout and reads each body unbounded (a stalled connection leaves the status on "Repairing" until a reload; add an `AbortSignal` and a size cap from the expected byte count); the registration update is awaited, so one that hangs holds the status; a newer worker installed by that update is activated by the stop screen's own update handler, which can reload the page after the status text (decide whether the stop screen should skip `update`); a write that fails partway leaves earlier entries replaced (the message says so, and each file was checked); the entry written carries only a content type, not the original headers; the waiting-build and other-tab repair case and an online-save-survives check are owed to 2b-4. Deferred from the 2a reviews (`/bmad-code-review` and `/gds-code-review`, record in `review-offline-package-slice2a-2026-10-20.md`): the load cost, since the verified loader compiles from bytes only after the download and the hash (no streaming overlap, and no URL-keyed code cache, so a repeat boot may compile again and run its first steps on baseline code), so the measurement must include a repeat boot from the precache and the time of the first steps, not only the cold first visit, and the perf harness boot-path probe now times `instantiate` from bytes where it used to time `instantiateStreaming` (compare #1005's cold-start figures with that in mind); the dev serve does not report `verified: false` on the boot state (design 5.3), and the production check is a scan for the skip's marker, so a skip written without it would pass (add a positive assertion that the loader chunk carries the check); the `controlled` flag in a worker comes from the worker's own `navigator`, with no test of that side; the two source-citing notes of design section 11 (cite the workbox-precaching lines, state that deploys are atomic alias flips); a note for the day a CSP lands, since the glue now loads from a `blob:` script; an engine that finishes compiling after the 90 s deadline stays in memory with the glue's module record for the page life (the streaming load did the same; compile first and check the signal before `init` would avoid the instance); the perf harness raw WASM mode (`pageSim.ts`) still imports the glue by URL, so it now builds its own unverified instance and its rows are not comparable with runs before 2.45.0; and `shellProblems` accepts any chunk that carries the package id, where design 5.2 item 4 wants the loader's and the engine worker's chunks (tighten it with the positive assertion). From the independent review's interim notes at 1b9b8c7: a `Content-Length` over the cap is coded `mismatch` though no body was read (say "declares N bytes", consider `load-failed`); `refuse()` (non-200, redirect, `text/html`) never sets `expected`, so `servedPackage` depends on which fetch finishes first; `shellProblems` compares only the package id, so a chunk with the right id but another build's compiled-in digests passes (assert `sha256(canonicalFilesJson(files))` equals the id); unit mutants that survive (no test puts the marker in `index.html`, none proves `verify-precache.ts` calls `shellProblems`, none proves boot hands `loadWasmEngine` the deadline signal, none pins a 2xx status other than 200); a worker-route tamper e2e and a `shellProblems` check that the main, engine worker and dual run worker chunks each carry the id; and HP-11, which one measured option could win back (hash a bounded `res.clone()` while `compileStreaming(res)` runs, and `init` only once the digest matches). Owed from the criterion 7 review (PR #1119; specs 1, 3 and 6 and deployment B's own bundle landed in slice 2a): a stalled and a 500 `version.json` in the e2e flow; the fixture's per-path overrides and a browser restart for design spec 5's cold start on B; watching `installing` turn `redundant` so a bad integrity names its cause instead of timing out; a timeout of its own on `registration.update()`; the no-telemetry half of offline play passes only because `telemetryHostAllowed()` is false on 127.0.0.1. Open behavior choice: the hourly backstop does not offer a waiting worker when `version.json` is missing (`isDifferentBuild(null)` is false; pinned in `src/pwa.test.ts`), while `onNeedRefresh` does; decide whether the backstop should offer it too. **#1345 (offline cold start after the precache is lost) is fixed in the shell integrity PR (2026-10-29):** every precache entry now carries `integrity` (`precacheIntegrityTransform` and `entryWithIntegrity`, `src/build/enginePackageBuild.ts`), so Workbox stores a miss and an online visit refills the shell as well as the engine; a navigation that fails its integrity falls back to the network without storing (`src/public/sw-navigation-fallback.js`); `verify-precache.ts` checks every digest; `e2e/engineEviction.spec.ts` covers the offline cold start after recovery and the failed-integrity navigation. Not healed by the first visit: files the page has not requested yet (a sound not yet played). |

| 2026-10-14 | wasm-worker-sync-serialize | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **Done (PR #1030; issue #1018 closes on its merge).** On the worker host the synchronous `serialize` callers (quick save, slot saves, the undo snapshot, the crash bundle, the pre-update flush, the TDT export) get the read model's own save while steps are in flight. | 2026-10-15 | wasm-worker-approx-save-order | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | superseded | **Superseded 2026-10-28 (backlog triage, closed as not planned, owner OK): the worker host is postponed with #1224 and contract PR 5 deletes the old host code; reopen if the code survives.** **Ready (deferred from the `/gds-code-review` of the #1018 fix, PR #1030).** A synchronous save taken with steps in flight on the worker host places commands made mid-flight before the in-flight steps the engine ran first, so a sale, build or rent change made while a batch crossed an hour or midnight applies before that boundary on load (one boundary of rent, upkeep or income can differ). Day-stamped fields and log lines the command wrote carry the later clock, a long stale streak widens the rollback past one batch, and an undo restored from a mid-flight snapshot shows the clock that much earlier. No data is lost. Options: settle first in the callers that can wait (quick and slot saves), or stamp mid-flight commands so a load replays them after the steps. Add coverage for the real callers (`SaveGame.packSync`, TDT export, crash bundle, undo through `adoptSim`), a forced stale streak and a watchdog timeout with steps pending. `/gds-code-review`. |

A clean refusal (the engine's `Result::Err`, a crowd, a foreign catalog) stays a refusal, and the engine a refusal built is freed now. Tested on the real engine with a trap injected at the binding boundary before `fromSave` and before the catalog read: the loaded tower and the next stay on TypeScript and no call reaches the module after the trap. A trap in the engine save writer is its own row (`wasm-writer-trap-latch`). The worker host needs none of it: a failure terminates its worker. `/gds-code-review`. |

Step 1 done: every test writes a start line to stderr from the worker (`src/tests/ci/testStartLogSetup.ts`, on with `VC_LOG_TEST_STARTS=1`), because vitest's reporter only learns of a test from the worker's event loop and a synchronous stall never reaches it; the `test:wasm` steps in `test.yml` and `engine-rs.yml` have a 12-minute limit, so a repeat ends in minutes and the log names the test. Reading it: the integration project runs files in parallel workers, so other workers keep writing start lines after a stall; the stuck test is the last start line of the file that never reported, and a file with no start line at all stalled in its imports or `beforeAll`. Follow-up, open (from the review of #1187, also a Codex P2): that kill uses a negative pid (a process group), which Node rejects on Windows, so on the owner's PC the 14 s scratch run is left running after the test; add a `process.platform === "win32"` branch (`child.kill()` or `taskkill /T /F`) and wait for the child to close, in `testStartLog.test.ts`. Step 2, open: find the cause on the next occurrence, or by looping the suite on a runner. `/bmad-code-review`. |

The small tower shows the same at its scale (the reloaded engine's build 1.1 ms against 0.7 on `main`, 2.56 to 4.00 MiB). The `perf:worker` harness reads M6 after 30 steps with no build, so this is outside M6 as measured, and every session that builds after a load reaches it; P1 since 2026-10-23 (the issue's label, raised with the owner's #1242 comment on the combined-branch memory reading). **Headroom taken 2026-10-29 (round 1 of #1259):** a loaded list keeps an eighth (at least 64) of room past its units and `Tower::add_unit` grows a full list by a quarter, so the first placements copy nothing (allocator-counted test `placing_after_a_large_load_copies_nothing`; a PC re-measure of 'after one build' is still owed) and a sustained build copies a block of 2.25 times the list at worst, not 3. **Update (2026-11-03, 2.78.4): a smaller `Unit` step.** The four retail tallies move out of line (`Unit::retail()` / `retail_mut()`, a box kept only while one is set) and the odd non-numeric `completeAt` value is boxed, so a `Unit` is 280 bytes natively (400 before; `a_unit_stays_small` pins it). Cloud `npm run perf:load-memory` on the large tower, 5 reps, Node package: WASM memory after the load 26.31 to 21.50 MiB, after a save 30.38 to 25.56 MiB; one build after a load grows nothing (26.31 and 21.50, the headroom round already did that). Cloud estimates, not the PC. **Update (2026-11-03, 2.78.7): step 3.** The retail tallies join `vacate_*`, `film_policy`, `complete_at*` and `dirty_days` in one out-of-line `Unit::extras` record (`extras()` reads, `extras_mut()` writes and drops the record when the last fact clears), so a `Unit` is 192 bytes natively (280 after step 2, 400 before; `a_unit_stays_small` pins it). Same cloud harness, large tower, 5 reps: WASM memory after the load 21.50 to 18.50 MiB (26.31 at the start of #1259 step 2), after a save 25.56 to 22.56 MiB; one build after a load still grows nothing. **Left:** the count fields (`customers_in`, `hotel_customers_in`, `out_for_meal`, `residents`: marked frame fields), `rent`, `label` and `subtype` (guarded by the marks and meta source scans) stay in the unit, and fixed-size chunks would stop growth past the headroom from copying the list; neither is started. Earlier left-over: a smaller `Unit` (#1243 (2)) or fixed-size chunks, so growth past the headroom never copies the whole list. Fix, measured first: a smaller `Unit` (#1243 (2)), or units in fixed-size chunks so growth never copies the whole list; reserved headroom only delays it. Placement stays amortized O(1); results identical. `/gds-code-review` and a hot-path record. **DONE (closes #1259 on merge), PC re-measure 2026-11-04** (`npm run perf:load-memory -- --reps 10`, Node package, engine sources `ae7409fc81a8861e`, main `8281eaee`, Windows PC, the Wine harness running alongside so build times are not clean): one floor tile built straight after a load grows nothing on any perf tower (large 18.50 to 18.50 MiB, medium 19.69 to 19.69, small 2.13 to 2.13) and the build takes 0.4 ms (large, median of 10), against 24.56 to 52.38 MiB and 10.6 ms in the report. Fixed-size unit chunks are not started and nothing measured calls for them. |

Differential tests (`export_stream_tests.rs`) compare it with the tree path on engine games, every fixture tower, every loader case and edited texts. What now sets the `.vctower` peak is the deflate's own buffers (its output is sized to its input), not the save. Found while measuring #1242. The game's engine writer (`setEngineWriter` in `bootstrap.ts`, set on any Rust boot, through `packVctower`) calls it for Quick Save, slot saves, the flush before a reload, export and the crash report, and for the autosave where the browser lacks `CompressionStream("deflate-raw")` (`saveToAsync` then falls back to `packSync`). On the large perf tower (cloud probe, 2026-10-27, on `main` at `511dadf6`, the build before #1225 and #1242's round 1 tree on it, 10 fresh processes, medians; the probe's source is on the issue), after a save that #1242 leaves at 28.63 MiB, the first `encodeVctower` takes WASM memory to 63.44 MiB in 161.9 ms, against 126.8 ms before #1225 (no growth, from 128.31) and 134.3 ms on `main` since #1225 (58.19 to 62.44 MiB); #1242's later call takes 116.3 ms against 104.7 and 106.5, with overlapping ranges, and grows nothing, where `main`'s grows 2.06 MiB, so the session's peak on this path stays within `main`'s range (63.44 against 62.44 to 64.50 MiB): 1.00 MiB above `main` after the first call and 1.06 under it after the second. That is about 23 MiB past the roughly 40 MiB a cloud estimate of M6 leaves the engine. `perf:worker` reads M6 after 30 steps with no Quick Save, so this sits outside M6 as measured, and every session that Quick Saves, saves to a slot or exports reaches it. Whether slice 9's budget of no save task over 50 ms on the large PC tower covers Quick Save is for slice 9's protocol; if it does, this writer alone is over it on every engine (104.7 to 161.9 ms on that probe), with or without #1242. Not taken with #1242: `encode_vctower` is a second writer with its own byte contract (the stamp rules and JavaScript's number spelling) and needs its own differential suite and review. Fix, measured first (add an `encodeVctower` step to `perf:load-memory`): write the `.vctower` payload without a whole-save tree, the same file byte for byte, with differential tests against today's `encode_vctower` on engine saves, loader cases and fixtures. `/gds-code-review` and a hot-path record. |

| 2026-10-21 | engine-load-memory-remainders | #43 | engine-rs | review-deferral | P1 | low | — | open | **Milestone 5.x, top priority (owner, 2026-11-19: 4.x is nearly done, so the Backlog and Triage sweep goes to the top of 5.x).** **Ready.** Deferred from the `/gds-code-review` of #1225, which streams only version 7 saves. (1) A save before version 7 still loads through `parse_save_json`, `migrate_save` and `deserialize`, a whole-save `Value` plus a copy per version step: the medium perf scenario (a version 4 save) peaks at 51.13 MiB of WASM memory on its first load (cloud, 2026-10-21 and 2026-10-27); since slice 5 a player's file goes to `fromSave` directly, so this is the medium scenario's 1.79x memory ratio in the 2026-10-28 PC run (WASM 51.13 MiB). **Done 2026-10-28 in the PR that migrates in place:** `deserialize_owned` and `migrate_save_owned` set the version keys in place, `with` stops cloning the keys it replaces, `units_of` borrows, and each unit entry is freed as it is converted; the medium tower's load peak went from 71.3 to 25.6 MiB and the version 1 tower's from 161 to 93 MiB (cloud allocator count, `load_memory_tests.rs`). Left: the version 1 re-lay (`reflow_v1_to_v2`) and the version 5 party hall step still build their own copies; streaming the older versions entry by entry would drop the remaining parsed-tree peak. Needs a PC re-measure of medium's WASM memory and adopt time. (2) `Unit` is 400 bytes native (336 in the WASM build) and most of a loaded game, and every unit owns a heap copy of its default label (each floor and lobby tile a "Floor" or "Lobby" string, cloned again by `reindex`; TypeScript shares one). Fix: move rarely set fields out of line and imply the default label; a smaller `Unit` also shrinks #1259's copy. (3) Each `units` entry is parsed into its own SipHash `IndexMap` before `unit_from_value` reads it, an estimated 25 to 45 ms of a large load of about 140 ms (cloud estimate, 2026-10-21); fix, if a native profile shows it matters, with a per-unit visitor that still reads unknown keys as `Value` (so `1e400` there is still refused). (4) The streamed reader reserves the unit vectors from a count of the objects `units` lists, before any entry converts: at most one unit per 24 bytes of text and `UNIT_CAP` in all, and a unit with its id takes 352 bytes in the WASM build, so up to about 28 MiB on a 2 MiB text. A hand-made save whose `units` lists objects that never become units reserves that much and keeps the memory for the session though it loads, and a text that turns out malformed inside `units` reserves it before the reader declines it; shipped hosts load only TypeScript's own re-save, so this matters once slice 5 sends a player's file to `fromSave` directly. The count's length bound uses the whole text, so padding outside `units` (a long name or log) lifts it; bounding it by the array's own span costs nothing and stays exact on saves the game writes. Fix: bound by the array's span, and reserve once the first entries convert, or in blocks. Results unchanged. (5) The save's text buffer starts at 128 bytes and doubles (#1242), so a save holds up to about three times its text for a moment. A buffer reserved from an estimate of the list lengths, written with `serde_json::to_writer`, would lower the high-water mark on large by at most the 4.06 MiB the save adds less the 2.06 MiB its text needs, about 2 MiB (an estimate, cloud, 2026-10-27); that matters once #1259 and #1338 bring a session's peak down to the save's. (6) Each unit's and transport's `Value` in the save (#1242) copies the unit's label, a `completeAt` that is not a number and the transport's skipped floors, which the loader keeps at any length, so a hand-made save can make one element's copy large (a log line's text, which its `Value` copies too, is capped at 400 UTF-16 units when it is loaded from a save; lines written in play are the game's and the host's own text); the save holds one at a time beside its text (found by #1242's review, round 6). Fix: a `Serialize` for `Unit` and `Transport` that writes from the borrow, with the same differential tests (the Hot-Path Auditor's N2 on #1242). No byte changes. `/gds-code-review` and a hot-path record. |

TypeScript's `dropOverlappingUnits` scans its rows the same way): fix with a bitset row per floor and layer (375 bits in six `u64` words), so a test or a claim is at most 18 word operations, the grids shrink from 82,500 to 10,560 bytes, and the first kept unit still wins. | 2026-10-21 | panel-answer-async-dialog | — | engine-rs | review-deferral | P2 | medium | — | superseded | **Superseded 2026-10-28 (engine triage on #1104; issue closed): the engine port answers in the same call (`engine-port-contract.md` section 1), so a panel answer cannot land after the player moved on. The cases below apply only to an async worker host, which the gateway design of slice 12 (#1224) takes up.** **Ready (Edge Case Hunter and Blind Hunter, facade slice 4 PR 4a review, `/gds-code-review` round 1 and `/bmad-code-review` rounds 2 and 3; not reachable on today's adapters).** Panels ask `port.query` and draw in the answer's callback. `TsSim` and the main-thread `RustSim` answer in a microtask of the same task, so nothing happens in between; an asynchronous host (the worker `RustWorkerSim`, slice 12, #1224) lets input land while the answer is out. Open cases: the answer reopens a dialog the player closed, or displaces a dialog that owns pending work (an emergency choice, a confirm), since the stats modal opens `displaceable`. 4a already drops an older answer (`latest`), an answer about a swapped-out tower, a second exterminator click, and a confirm for a tower since replaced. Also on an async host: the stats screen reads the header and the tower name at draw time, so they can be a few steps newer than the report; and an exterminator ask that never settles keeps that tower's button from opening a confirm until a load. Remedy: a cheap "has the player moved on" check the dialog layer offers (a modal generation counter bumped on every open and close, plus `precedence.ownsPendingWork`), taken at ask time and tested at draw time, plus a timeout on an ask that holds a button, before an async host carries panels. Gate: slice 12's tests close the panel (or open a protected dialog) before the answer lands and see no reopen. **Also (2026-10-27, facade slice 4e-1, Edge Case Hunter and Hot-Path Auditor):** the traffic chip asks the port every UI tick through `inOrder` (`src/simport/latest.ts`), which never draws an older answer after a newer one and holds nothing back. A port that answers more slowly than the UI tick (about 6 Hz) would queue a full congestion build per tick, keep a replaced tower reachable until its asks settle (forever if the port stalls), and draw the backlog in one burst. Remedy: cap the asks still out per app at a small number, or ask only when the oldest one is from an earlier task. A strict one-in-flight gate is wrong: under the stepped screenshot clock several `TsSim` asks are legitimately out within one task, and skipping them would change the hysteresis path the gallery captures. Two more on such a port (round 3 review): the tooling's `refreshTraffic` can settle without drawing when a newer frame-loop ask overtakes it, even one that failed (that failure goes to the frame loop's log and the capture passes), and a failure that lands after a newer ask settled is dropped without a trace. Gate: a test with a port that never settles, one where the tooling's ask is overtaken (it must draw or reject), and a count of dropped failures. |

## Deferral inbox

The review skills append each `defer` finding here, under a dated `### Deferred from:` heading. The PR that parks it folds it into a curated row before it merges.

## History from Verticopolis/web

The deferral notes, triage records and follow-ups below collected in Verticopolis/web's backlog before this repository had its own. They are kept as they were written, under the headings they sat under there.

## Web's deferral inbox

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of Modern rental living (2026-07-23)

- **[TRIAGED 2026-07-28 -> absorbed into #324 (finance-1010).]** Rental rent income banks to the "condos" ledger line (Blind + Edge + Acceptance, minor, by-design). `collectMonthlyRent` (`src/engine/economy/rentalIncome.ts`) and the rental operating overhead both record against the `"condos"` financial-breakdown category (`ledgerCatFor` maps both rental kinds there), so recurring rental cashflow is commingled with one-time condo-sale proceeds in the finance readout. This honors the GDD constraint "residential ledger line, like the condo" (no new ledger category), so it is intended, not a defect. Legibility polish if picked up: add a dedicated `"rentals"` `LedgerCat` (a new line + `LEDGER_CATS` entry + `ledgerCatFor` rental cases) so a player can separate rent income from condo sales. Deferred because it widens the ledger vocabulary beyond this feature's "reuse the existing ledger" scope. **Absorbed** rather than given its own row: #324 already plans exactly this additive `LedgerCat` extension for the finance taxonomy, so a separate rentals line would compete with it. The income loop now sums per category off each unit's own kind, so the split needs only the new cat, not a rewrite.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of hotel late-checkout (#304 Phase 1, 2026-07-20)

- **[TRIAGED 2026-11-15 -> row `hotel-late-checkout-destroyed-rent`, #2283.]** **Deferred rent is lost if a late-checkout room is destroyed between 08:00 and 14:00 (Edge Case Hunter + Blind Hunter, minor).** Modern's morning `hotelCheckout()` skips booking rent for the deferred rooms; `hotelLateCheckout()` books it at 14:00. If a deferred room is bulldozed or catches fire in that window, its rent is never booked, a small revenue delta versus the pre-feature single 08:00 checkout (which had already collected it). Narrow and arguably fair (the player destroyed an occupied room), and bounded by `round(0.2 * N)` rooms. Fix shape if it ever matters: book the deferred rent at the morning checkout and only defer the room STATE, or clear the deferral (book immediately) in the bulldoze/fire path. Not worth a serialized field for a rare, self-inflicted case. All three review layers otherwise confirmed the guardrails hold (determinism, revenue-once under normal play, bounded non-duplicating census, housekeeping lifecycle).

## Triaged (2026-07-21, #545)

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of Modern smart dispatch (housekeeping-overhaul epic 4, PR #463) (adversarial, 2026-07-17)

> **ABSORBED + RETIRED (#545).** The triage travel proxy (blind to transport quality and crew saturation) folds into `housekeeping-modern-refinements` (#547). The `crowd/spawn.ts` and `gameRules.ts` 500-line file-size heads-ups are informational, owned by `fileSize.guard.test.ts` (its own CI gate), not defers: retired.

- **The triage travel proxy is blind to transport quality and crew saturation (Blind Hunter + Acceptance Auditor, low; refinement with the playtest weight pass).** The score's travel term is floor distance to the NEAREST staff-connected crew: a six-floor stairs-only climb scores like a six-floor service-elevator ride, and when the nearest crew's six maids are all out, a much farther crew is the one that actually travels while the score still reads "close". Documented as an accepted v1 proxy in the GDD's pinned-weight note; revisit alongside the `MODERN_HK_TRIAGE` weight tuning if playtest shows Modern towers mis-prioritizing (fix shapes: distance to the nearest crew WITH a free maid, or a transport-aware cost off the staff route length).
- **`crowd/spawn.ts` sits at exactly the 500-line file-size ceiling (staff-pool fix 2026-07-17, informational).** Same hazard as the `gameRules.ts` row below: the next added line trips the guard mid-feature. Natural split candidates when next touched: the meal/venue option-pool builders, or the staff spawn path (`spawnStaff`/`maxStaffFor`/`takeStaffResults`) into a `staff.ts` leaf mirroring `trips.ts`/`visits.ts`.
- **`gameRules.ts` sits at exactly the 500-line file-size ceiling (Edge Case Hunter, informational).** The next added line trips `fileSize.guard.test.ts` mid-feature. Plan the next cohesive split (candidates: the Modern deeper-economy layer, or the two rule-set literals) BEFORE the next GameRules seam lands, mirroring the `households.ts`/`pricing.ts` precedents, rather than splitting under pressure inside an unrelated PR.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of time-simulated maids (housekeeping-overhaul epic 2, PR #461) (adversarial, 2026-07-17)

> **ABSORBED + RETIRED (#545).** The capacity verdict lying below ~114 rooms/crew (the medium finding) folds into `housekeeping-modern-refinements` (#547). Retired: the `MAX_STAFF` pool cap was RESOLVED in v1.57.0 (`maxStaffFor(tower)`); the tick-granularity throughput sensitivity is inherent and informational; the `onResult` stale-token desync is test-model-only (v1 coarse ticks, provably closed under production v2); and the save-after-cutoff lost-cleaning-day is inherent to the transient crowd and gated on a playtest signal.

- **The nominal-capacity shortfall verdicts compare demand to an unreachable best case and are now ~5.7x too optimistic (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter, medium; epic 3 charter).** `housekeepingCoverage.dailyCapacity` is `crews * 6 * 19 = 114/crew` (was 20), but real throughput is travel-dominated (car speed 0.8 floors/min; every job is a fresh one-way trip from the crew floor; one maid per floor per unit), so a basement crew serving 90 rooms on floors 20-28 reads GREEN (114 > 90) while actually clearing ~40-55/day and infesting. The red verdicts in `housekeepingDiagnostics.ts` (`dailyCapacity < rooms - infested`) and `stats.ts` (`hkShort`) are effectively dead below 114 rooms/crew. This is exactly the "the 'enough housekeeping' verdict stops lying" item in the overhaul GDD's epic 3 (legibility layer), queued next; the fix should key the verdict on OBSERVED shortfall (yesterday's leftover / rooms that survived a shift) rather than any nominal constant.
- **[RESOLVED 2026-07-17 (v1.57.0)] The global `MAX_STAFF = 64` pool caps ~10 housekeeping units' worth of maids (Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor, informational).** A real tower hit it: a 395-room save with 22 crews saturated the 64-maid pool all shift (~295 rooms/day cleaned against ~344 bookable), so a fully de-infested hotel re-infested within 4 days. Canon research confirmed the 1994 game has NO tower-wide staff pool (each housekeeping room fields its own 6 maids; documented near-max towers run 12-16 units per hotel section) and no build cap on housekeeping rooms. Fixed by replacing the constant with `maxStaffFor(tower)` (operational crews x `HK_MAIDS_PER_UNIT`) in the single consumer (`spawn.ts`), so the spawn gate can never bind below built capacity; the per-crew ledgers remain the real constraint. Original: No per-unit cap exists (spec satisfied), but an 11th+ unit's maids can be pool-starved into transient "full" retries on a very large hotel tower. Single consumer (`spawn.ts`); raise or make it scale with built units when a real tower hits it.
- **Cleaned-per-day throughput is tick-granularity sensitive (Acceptance Auditor, informational; inherent).** Staff results drain once per `advanceStep` and crowd motion is bounded by `CROWD_MAX_STEP`, so coarse steps quantize the maid cycle: the same hotel measured 14.8 rooms/maid at 1-minute ticks vs 11.0 at 10-minute ticks. Deterministic for a given tick script (golden hashes stable), and the arrival-cleaned model had the same property, but daily cleaned counts are now an economy input that varies with step size. Record as a known property; revisit only if v1-model coarse-tick consumers surface a real discrepancy.
- **`onResult` releases assignments by room id with no maid identity; a stale result can free a fresh assignment under `simModel = "v1"` coarse ticks (Edge Case Hunter, low; test-model-only).** Production v2 sub-steps (<=30 min) provably drain every result hours before the next `resetShift`, so the path is closed there; under v1 (test contexts, >=~165-minute ticks) a dilated trip can outlive the reset and its late result frees the NEW maid's floor slot / decrements the new crew's counter, desynchronizing the ledgers for the day (counters clamp at 0, no crash). Fix shape if it ever bites: stamp a monotonic dispatch token on the person and assignment, echo it through `staffDone`, ignore mismatches in `onResult`. 2026-07-17 addendum (staff-pool fix review, Edge Case Hunter, low, same v1-only window): a stale maid outliving `resetShift` also still counts against the now-crew-derived spawn ceiling, so on a small-crew v1 tower she can gate the next shift's opening wave one send early until her result drains; same root cause, same token fix shape.
- **A save loaded after the Classic 16:30 cutoff forfeits the rest of that day's cleaning (Edge Case Hunter, low; inherent to the transient crowd).** In-flight maids (and mid-dwell cleans) vanish on load and no dispatch can start until the next noon, so a save-at-16:29 habit walks rooms toward `INFEST_DAYS`. The old 8-19 model re-dispatched after reload until 19:00, so this is new pressure specific to the canon cutoff. The zero-clean morning report (patched in this same review) makes the lost day legible; escalate only if playtest shows it punishing ordinary save habits.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of weather-shapes-crowd (#430) (adversarial, 2026-07-16)

> **RETIRED (#545).** Making retail patronage crowd-emergent (rather than the surviving `rainMult`) is a demand-pool rearchitecture explicitly out of scope for a P3 texture pass; `commercial-demand-pools` (#393) already shipped the statistical model, so if a playtest ever wants rain to visibly starve shops the direction lives with the open `demand-pools-calibration` (#548) retune, not a standing row here. The attendance rain magnitude, the `weatherFor` layering smell, and the one-tick day-boundary weather lag are provisional/cosmetic and fold into the general playtest weight-tuning.

- **Retail rain stays a bolt-on multiplier, not emergent from the thinner crowd (Acceptance Auditor, med by intent, low by impact; scope).** The issue asks that "retail patronage and attendance fill drop because fewer people show up." This ships that for ATTENDANCE (thinner crowd lowers `customersIn`, `rainMult` dropped there), but retail income is statistical (`computeDemandMap` reads the connected census, not the drawn crowd), so thinning the crowd does not touch retail income and rain reaches retail only through the surviving `rainMult`. Making retail patronage crowd-emergent is a demand-pool rearchitecture (route the pool through delivered foot traffic), out of scope for this P3 texture pass. Recorded so the issue's fuller "one source of truth" ambition stays tracked; revisit under `demand-pools-calibration` (#548) if a playtest wants rain to visibly starve shops through their emptier aisles rather than a multiplier (`commercial-demand-pools` #393 shipped and closed).
- **The attendance rain magnitude is now emergent and non-linear, not the old clean 0.5, and nothing pins it (Blind Hunter, low; calibration).** A rainy attendance house used to take a flat x0.5; it now takes whatever fill reduction the thinned spawn stream produces, which is non-linear in the spawn rate (seat-cap saturation, elevator throughput). `rainCrowdFactor` 0.5/0.7 are PROVISIONAL (flagged in `econConfig.ts`); the end-to-end test asserts direction only (`rainMinutes < clearMinutes`), never a magnitude. Fold a magnitude calibration (does a rainy cinema land near the intended take?) into the same playtest tuning pass as the other provisional crowd/economy weights.
- **The crowd-only spawn path still recomputes `weatherFor(clock.day)` and imports it from `sim/build` (Edge Case Hunter, low; layering smell).** The production loop now passes `sim.weather` (single source shared with the economy), but `spawnStep`'s fallback for the no-Simulation paths (`motion.update`, crowd-driven tests) still derives weather from the day hash, keeping a `crowd/spawn.ts` -> `sim/build.ts` import. Not circular (verified: `sim/build` imports `Simulation` type-only plus leaves), just a leaf living next to `build`/`sellAt`. Hoist `weatherFor` into a dedicated leaf (`weather.ts`) that both `sim/build` and `crowd/spawn` import if the smell is worth a follow-up.
- **Crowd and economy weather can lag the true day by one tick at a day boundary, though they now agree (Edge Case + Blind Hunter, low; cosmetic).** `sim.weather` is refreshed in `onDay`, after `onHour`/`collectTrafficIncome` in the same step, so the midnight-crossing tick's spawns and income both read yesterday's weather for that one hour. The patch made the two layers read the SAME value (so they no longer DISAGREE, which was the real bug), but both lag by one step until `onDay` latches the new day. Reordering the `sim.weather` refresh ahead of the hour reads would remove the lag; deferred as cosmetic (at most one hour per day change, self-correcting) rather than risk reordering `onDay`.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of elevator-schedule-model (#305 Phase 1) (adversarial, 2026-07-16)

> **ABSORBED (#545) into the `elevator-scheduling` epic (#305).** Both items are sub-concerns of that epic: `setCars` not re-coercing an in-memory schedule is a Phase 2/3 dispatch-clamp concern (a save/load self-heals it today), and the provisional `WAITING_CAR_RESPONSE_MAX`/`STANDARD_FLOOR_DEPARTURE_MAX` clamp bounds reconcile with the Phase 3 stepper ranges when that spec lands.

- **`setCars` does not resize or re-coerce an in-memory schedule; Phase 2 dispatch must clamp against the live car count (Edge Case Hunter, low; Phase 2 concern).** `Tower.setCars` (`tower/transport.ts`) mutates `cars`/`carPositions`/`carDir` but leaves an authored `schedule` untouched, so after a player shrinks a shaft's cars (Phase 3 UI) an authored `homeFloors` can be longer than the new car count and `activeCars` rows can hold values above it. A save/load self-heals it (`coerceSchedule` re-clamps against the new `cars`), and Phase 1 never reads the schedule, so it is inert now. Fix belongs in Phase 2: the dispatch read must clamp active-car and home-floor indices against the live `cars`, not the stored schedule length (and Phase 3's `setCars` path may re-coerce the schedule on the spot). Recorded so the Phase 2 dispatch story picks it up.
- **The `WAITING_CAR_RESPONSE_MAX` (30) / `STANDARD_FLOOR_DEPARTURE_MAX` (60) clamp bounds are PROVISIONAL and not yet validated against the spec's stated stepper ranges (Acceptance Auditor, note; calibration).** The arch/gdd (in PR #452, not yet on main) own the real ranges; these leaf bounds are a defensive trust-boundary clamp, marked provisional in `elevatorSchedule.ts`. Reconcile them with the Phase 3 UI stepper ranges when that PR lands, widening if calibration needs more room. No code defect; a bounds-vs-spec cross-check deferred until the spec doc is on main.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of elevator-dispatch-schedule (#305 Phase 2) (adversarial, 2026-07-17)

> **ABSORBED + RETIRED (#545).** The off-shift car abandoning an in-cab rider and the absolute-vs-comparative `waitingCarResponse` mapping are Phase 3 concerns of the `elevator-scheduling` epic (#305), reachable only once the dialog can author an active-car cut. The `dwellMinutesFor` vs arch `dwellSecondsFor` naming/unit divergence is a doc-only nit (behavior is correct): retired.

- **An off-shift car abandons an in-cab rider mid-trip instead of delivering them first (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor; low, under-specified interaction).** When a scheduled active-car count drops at an hour boundary, a car whose index becomes `>= activeCount` is parked before the cab-call scan and its statistical `carLoad` is zeroed, matching the GDD's "off-shift cars carry no statistical load". A real drawn rider aboard (state `riding`, `carIndex === i`) then gets no forced cab stop; the car drifts home, and the rider only alights if that drift crosses their destination floor, else the existing give-up valve (`crowd/motion.ts` patience cull) despawns them as frustrated. This is the SAME safety path as a bulldozed shaft or a `setCars` trim, so no rider is stranded forever and routing/reachability is untouched (invariant GDD 6.3 holds); the ungraceful part is the frustration spike on a routine scheduled shift-change. A "deliver current cab riders, then park" drain (an off-shift car serves only its own cab stops until empty, answering no new hall/statistical calls) would avoid punishing mid-ride passengers. Deferred to Phase 3: the interaction is only reachable once the dialog lets a player author an active-car cut, and it wants an end-to-end (crowd + dispatch) test then, not a dispatch-only one now.
- **Classic resolved in #2049 (2026-11-12, owner ruling "fix the dispatch in classic"): Classic now reads Waiting Car Response as the SimTower wiki describes it (a parked car leaves a call to a car already heading there within that many floors, `rules::elevators`); the reach gate below stays as Modern's reading until the Modern dispatch party round decides.** **Modern resolved in #2057 (2026-11-12, owner picked the party round's smart dispatch package): the reach gate is retired from both modes; Modern gives each call to the best-placed car and folds Waiting Car Response into that choice (a car already heading there within that many floors keeps the call), which is the comparative reading below. Only the test-only plain SCAN profile keeps the gate, for the hashed reference test.** **Waiting Car Response is an absolute span-distance gate, not the spec's comparative "closer than the currently-assigned car" threshold (Acceptance Auditor; medium by spec text, low by impact; provisional mapping).** GDD §4.1/§4.2.3 define `waitingCarResponse` relative to the already-assigned car, but our SCAN core (`claimed` set + `nextDemandStop`) has no per-car assignment-cost function to compare against, so Phase 2 maps it onto an absolute `reach = span - response` hold for a parked car (flagged "Provisional mapping" in `elevatorSchedule.ts`/`ElevatorDispatch.ts`). It preserves the stepper's direction (higher = stays put longer) and the no-op default, and answers a within-reach call in either scan direction. Reconcile the exact geometry with the Phase 3 stepper range and, if the SCAN core ever grows a per-car assignment cost, revisit the literal comparative semantics. Pairs with the provisional-bounds defer above.
- **Arch names the dwell helper `dwellSecondsFor`; the implementation is `dwellMinutesFor` returning game-minutes (Acceptance Auditor; nit, naming only).** The impl divides the stored game-seconds by 60 to match the dispatcher's `DWELL_MINUTES` unit, which is behaviorally correct and clearer at the call site; the divergence is only the helper's name and unit vs the architecture doc. Reconcile the arch §2/§3 wording with the shipped helper in the next doc-touch pass; no code change warranted.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of contiguous-skylobby-transfer (#396) (adversarial, 2026-07-16)

> **RETIRED (#545).** The silently-retroactive gate on pre-1.48 Classic saves was a one-time rollout window now long past (we are at v1.70+), and real-save validation stranded zero floors; the in-PR advisory/copy rewording already shipped. The dispatch still serving gate-stranded floors and the floor-level (not tile-contiguous) gate are documented deliberate v1 simplifications; they resurface with a future reachability-unification pass, not worth a standing row now.

- **The gate is silently retroactive on pre-1.48 Classic saves (Edge Case Hunter, med; rollout edge of an intended rule).** A Classic tower built around a plain-floor express endpoint acting as a transfer hub loads in 1.48.0 with those upper floors flipped to unreachable: commercial income there stops, move-ins stop, sitting tenants are grandfathered on `isFloorServed` rent, so the tower decays quietly. The strand risk was the named acceptance concern and the rule is canon, so the behavior itself is intended; what is deferred is a friendlier rollout surface: a load-time advisory when a just-loaded Classic tower has gate-stranded floors, or a "what changed" line in the update modal beyond the Player-note. Partially mitigated in-PR: the stranded-floor advisory, stats legend, and inspector access line were reworded from the false "3+ rides" to the accurate "no route from the lobby within two rides", and the daily nudge plus inspector line do fire for gate-stranded floors. Real-save validation: a 100-floor player tower with sky lobbies every 15 floors strands zero floors.
- **Elevator dispatch still serves gate-stranded floors (Edge Case Hunter, low; pre-existing divergence class, widened).** `ElevatorDispatch` gates statistical waiting demand on `servedFloors()` only, so occupied units on floors the router treats as unreachable (3+ rides before this PR; now also Classic gate-stranded floors) keep accruing demand and cars keep visiting, while income, move-ins, and the drawn crowd all agree the floor draws no one. Same class as the known `servedFloors`-vs-two-ride divergence (`servedFloors` ignores MAX_RIDES too); fold into any future reachability-unification pass rather than a bespoke dispatch patch here.
- **v1 gates at FLOOR level, not tile-contiguous lobby spans (deliberate simplification, documented in `route()`).** The 1994 notion is a contiguous lobby span touching both shafts on the transfer floor; v1 admits the transfer if the floor has any lobby tile (ground floor 1 always). A lobby tile far from both shafts thus admits a transfer canon would refuse. Revisit only if a playtest shows towers gaming it; the fix needs per-shaft x positions against the lobby tile spans on the floor.

### Deferred from: gds-code-review of deserialize-coercion (adversarial, 2026-07-16)

> **ABSORBED (#545) into `deserialize-coercion` (#306).** Both findings are the same save-hardening seam: a forged huge-but-finite `minutes` (past ~2^53) freezes the clock via the `+=` precision trap (fix: a `CLOCK_MINUTES_CAP` alongside the finite/non-negative clamp, matching the existing `VIP_VISITS_CAP`/`ID_CAP`), and a forged huge-but-finite `money` can re-introduce `Infinity` on the next ledger add (money has no canonical ceiling, so it stays a designer question). They were out of scope for #306's original non-finite fix but belong to that coercion story.

- **A forged huge-but-finite `minutes` (at or past ~2^53) still freezes the clock via the `+=` precision trap (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter, med; out-of-scope for #306).** The minutes coercion guards NaN/Infinity/negative/non-number but does not upper-bound magnitude, so a forged finite value like `1e18` is accepted verbatim; then `Clock.advance` does `this.minutes += dtMinutes` every tick, and at that magnitude the float ulp exceeds a normal step, so the clock never advances (day/quarter/year stall, hour/day boundary handlers never fire). This is the same 2^53 increment trap the file already caps for `VIP_VISITS_CAP` and `ID_CAP`, so minutes is the inconsistent one. Out of scope here: issue #306 is the non-finite/negative class, and this change does not worsen the pre-existing raw behavior (an uncapped huge minutes passed before too). Fix by adding a `CLOCK_MINUTES_CAP` (well above any real tower age, comfortably below 2^53) alongside the finite/non-negative clamp when this seam is next touched.
- **A forged huge-but-finite `money` (near `Number.MAX_VALUE`) passes the finite check and can re-introduce `Infinity` on the next ledger add (Blind Hunter, med/low; out-of-scope for #306).** `Number.isFinite(1.79e308)` is true, so it loads as-is; the first `money + cost` that overflows yields `Infinity`, the non-finite state the fix exists to prevent. Deliberately not clamped here: the spec (#306) asks only for a finite money, and a magnitude cap risks clipping a legitimately large late-game fortune (money has no canonical ceiling, unlike star/vipVisits). Record only; revisit with a designer if a money ceiling is ever wanted.

### Deferred from: gds-code-review of dispatch-shaft-fairness (adversarial, 2026-07-16)

> **RETIRED (#545).** The section records its own outcome: no findings deferred (1 patch fixed in-branch, 5 dismissed with evidence, 0 defer). Nothing to track.

- **No findings deferred.** All three adversarial layers ran against the isolated diff (branch `claude/dispatch-shaft-fairness`, #303). Triage: 1 patch, 5 dismiss, 0 defer.
  - PATCH (fixed in-branch): **`route()` became rng-impure, but `floorReachable`'s pure reachability probe (which discards the route and runs on the ~6 Hz editor repaint pump) called it, so UI timing could perturb the seeded crowd stream on a banked tower (Edge Case Hunter, major).** Fixed by adding a pure `crowd.reachable` (bfsRoute only, no balancing draw) and switching `floorReachable` to it; a regression test asserts the probe draws no rng even on a banked tower.
  - DISMISS: balanceShafts could pick a shaft the routing graph excluded (Blind Hunter, major) - impossible: `staffOnly` is uniformly determined by `kind` (only `elevatorService` is staff-only), so same-kind matching is a strict subset of both graphs' include predicates, and `stopsAt(both)` guarantees a real adjacency edge. The `!chosen` guard is defensive (Blind Hunter, minor) - ids come from live `bfsRoute` edges, kept as a non-crashing guard consistent with the module's other leg-lookup guards. rng stream is now data-dependent (Blind Hunter, minor) - intended per the spec ("changes the rng stream"); after the probe-purity patch only real rider trips draw, and determinism holds (round-trip golden test green). "Equivalent" scoped to same kind, so a standard-vs-express bank is not co-balanced (Acceptance Auditor, minor) - deliberate and documented (preserves transport mode and the service-first tie-break). Determinism test builds both towers in identical shaft order (Acceptance Auditor, minor) - the id-sort correctness is self-evident and the run-to-run test covers the stated purpose; optional hardening only.
  - PR review follow-up (Copilot, addressed in-branch): the per-leg bank scan was replaced by a `Tower.revision`-cached bank index (`shaftBanks`, keyed `kind:from:to`) so balancing is O(1) per leg; the dispatch test fixture now asserts every `place()` result.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of condo-demographic-routines (#397) (adversarial, 2026-07-16)

> **RETIRED (#545).** All four are provisional-weight, texture, or v1-loop items and measured non-issues on a real 100-floor save (crowd peaked 63/140, at most 3 concurrent callers): the sales-call cap pressure, the school-run statistical asymmetry, the no-op routine spawn pass, and the coarse v1-tick window skip. They ride the routines calibration pass, itself gated on a playtest signal; no standing row.

- **Sales-call dwellers pool in the ground lobby and could pressure the MAX_PEOPLE cap in very populous towers (Edge Case Hunter, med by analysis, low by measurement; calibration).** Structurally there is no throttle beyond one visible occupant per office, and the shared 140-person crowd cap hard-stops spawns, so a tower with hundreds of staffed offices could in theory hold tens of stationary floor-1 dwellers through the 10-15 window and starve lunch spawns. Measured on a real 100-floor, 22k-unit player save, the effect did not materialize: crowd peaked at 63/140 with at most 3 concurrent callers, so the analytic worst case overestimates how often the 0.35 weight wins the shared spawn pool. The weights are PROVISIONAL; fold a cap-pressure check (and any needed per-window throttle) into the routines calibration pass rather than pre-tuning against a scenario the data does not show.
- **School-run waves are statistically asymmetric and contend for the single weekday-daytime condo occupant (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter, low; texture).** Returns are decoupled from departures by design (no identity), so afternoon returns are not conserved against morning departures (a save loaded at 14:50 still gets returns; a cap-throttled morning still gets a full 15:00 wave); during 7-8 the in-flight child holds `outForMeal`, so the condo's breakfast meal trips and further school picks pause until the lobby arrival, and one single-occupant condo can emit several "children" across the hour while the stay-home adult visibly vanishes for each walk out. All within the statistical no-identity mandate and mostly invisible at normal zoom; revisit only if playtesting reads it as wrong, in the same calibration pass as the weights.
- **A picked routine option can no-op and consume its spawn pass (Blind Hunter, low; statistical dampening).** Both spawners pick a floor before filtering to eligible units and return silently when none qualifies, so an in-window pass that drew the routine option can produce nothing, slightly suppressing competing meal/commute options during the windows. Harmless at current weights; fold any restructuring (filter before push) into the calibration pass.
- **The legacy v1 sim model can skip the 1-hour routine windows on coarse catch-up ticks (Edge Case Hunter, low; pre-existing class).** v1 advances the clock wholesale and samples spawn once per tick, so a single 7:00-to-9:00 tick emits no school run at all; the v2 loop's hour-boundary substeps are immune. The same limitation already applies to the wider meal windows; these are just the narrowest. Belongs to any future v1-loop retirement or substepping pass, not a bespoke fix here.

### Deferred from: gds-code-review of pricing-split (#299) (adversarial, 2026-07-16)

A player parking hotel rooms or condos off-market sees that only in the editor/diagnostics.
